- Given a call in flight whose answer keeps arriving for longer than its timeout x attempts (a streamed answer, Q-60), when another call reserves its worst case, then the first call's reservation still counts against every cap until the first call ends: the budget guard renews it every third of its lifetime while the call runs, and when the call answers, fails or is aborted it settles or releases the reservation exactly once, after its last renewal (P3-99).
- Given a call in flight whose reservation cannot be renewed (DEC-162), when a renewal fails, then the failure is logged and the renewal is tried again after a short back-off, while the reservation still counts; when no renewal has succeeded by the time less than a sixth of the reservation's lifetime is left, the call is stopped with `RST-1604`, booked with what is known (a ledger row with the usage known so far if it was sent, else its reservation is released), exactly once, before the reservation can lapse; a reservation of a call that no longer runs (the server stopped) expires after its lifetime.
- Given no cap configured, when a run with a paid provider starts, then the default run cap from the project settings template applies; with server keys the effective run cap is the lower of the run's cap and the administrator's run cap (RF-1106, DEC-107).
  Verification: integration tests with the fake provider; unit tests of the cap source of a refused call (P3-91).

**RF-423 Cap per role** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-13 - ADR-007
As a developer I want a separate cap per role so that the verifier cannot consume the readers' budget.

- Given role caps, when a role reaches its cap, then only that role's calls stop.
  Verification: integration test.

**RF-424 Live cost meter** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-13 - ADR-007
As a developer I want to see tokens and cost grow during the run so that I can interrupt it.

- Given a run open in the web UI, when calls complete, then the meter updates with tokens and cost so far, the cap, and the share used (RF-1003).
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events.

**RF-425 Estimate before running** - R1 (M1: first scenario, DEC-97) - Must - Source DEC-13, Q-51 - ADR-007
As a user I want an estimate before a run so that I know the cost before spending.

- Given a code map and settings, when the start page loads or I press Re-estimate, then it shows the expected tokens and cost per role as a low, likely and high range, with the assumptions used.
- Given roles on a local model (Q-51, default applies), when the start page shows the estimate, then those roles show tokens and the sentence about the cap is written in the unit of the cap it is compared with; priced roles keep dollars; counts read "1 area", "2 areas".
- Given a request with no area list (P3-123), when the estimate is made, then its figures are those of the default selection (the own code when the project has library areas) while the list of areas still names every area, so the picker can offer the library areas unticked.
- Given an `understand` run whose likely estimate exceeds the configured confirmation threshold, when I start it, then the page shows the estimate and asks me to confirm.
  Verification: unit tests on the estimator; RNF-012 measures accuracy.

**RF-426 Cost report** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-13 - ADR-007
As a developer I want a cost report saved with every run so that I can compare providers and runs.

- Given any run that made model calls, when it ends, then `cost.md` and `cost.json` list tokens (input, output, cached) and cost by role, agent task, provider and model, with totals and the price table version.
  Verification: snapshot test.

**RF-427 Unpriced models** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-13 - ADR-007
As a developer I want a warning when a paid model has no price so that the cap cannot be bypassed by a missing price.

- Given a non-local model missing from the price table, when the run starts, then it stops with an error code unless the project setting `budget.allowUnpriced` is on and the role uses the user's own key, in which case cost is shown as unknown and only the token cap applies; server keys never run an unpriced model (DEC-112).
  Verification: integration test.

**RF-428 Project cost ledger** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-13, DEC-49 - ADR-007, ADR-012
As a developer I want the tokens and cost of every run on a project added up so that I always know what analysing it has cost in total.

- Given any model call, when it completes, then one row with user, project, run, stage, role, provider, model, key source (user or server), tokens (input, output, cached) and cost is inserted in the `cost_ledger` table in the same step as the budget check, so the totals survive an interrupted or failed run.
- Given the ledger, when I open a project's cost page, then it shows the project totals and a breakdown by run, stage, role, provider and model; local models show tokens with a cost of zero.
- Given a price table change, when totals are shown, then each line keeps the cost computed at the time of the call and the price table version it used.
- Given a run in a breakdown on the cost page, when the page shows it, then the run is named as the History page names it, and by its run id when History cannot be read or no longer lists the run (P8-90, UX-19).
- Given a table on the cost page, when the page shows the price table version, then the version is named once above the table, and in a column only when the table's rows used different versions (P8-90, UX-19).
- Given a run in the breakdown by run, when the cost route answers, then the run line carries its own `byRole`: one line per role that made calls in that run, in code-unit order, each with the tokens and cost of that role in that run; a role with no calls in the run has no line (it reads as none, never as a cost of zero); and, per currency, the known costs of the role lines add up to the run's total, as do their calls and tokens. Only the signed-in user's own ledger rows are counted (DEC-217, P8-108).
- Given a run in the breakdown by run, when the cost page shows it, then the row names its Kind in History's words (`Scan`, `Understand`, `Plan`, and `Deepen, 3 rounds` for a Deepen that has finished rounds), and a run History cannot name (History unreadable, or no longer listing the run) and the calls of no run (Plan setup options) read `-` in the Kind column (P8-109, DEC-211).
- Given the breakdown by run, when the cost page shows it, then each run row has one column for each of the four roles (Reader, Verifier, Planner, Summariser) and a Total column; a role that made no calls in the run reads `-`, never a cost of zero, and the exact amounts of the role cells of a row (the figures in their tooltips) add up to its Total; the figures shown, rounded to four decimals, may differ from it by that rounding (P8-109, P8-133, DEC-211).
- Given the breakdown by run, when the cost page shows it, then a last row, Project total, shows the project's total from the page's totals under the Total column, and the Total cells of the run rows add up to it (P8-109, DEC-211).
- Given the breakdown by run with role columns and a Project total row, when the cost page shows the amounts of that table, then each follows the money, tooltip and one-tab-stop scenarios above, and the table keeps its name `By run` (P8-109, P8-110, P8-117).
- Given any money amount on the cost page, when it is shown, then it follows DEC-54 (four decimals below $1, two from $1) and carries the exact figure in a tooltip (P8-90, EV-94).
- Given a money amount of exactly zero, when it is shown, then it reads `$0.00`, never `$0.0000` (DEC-217, P8-110).
- Given a money amount above zero and below $0.00005, when it is shown, then it reads `<$0.0001` and the tooltip carries the exact figure, so it never reads as zero (DEC-217, P8-110).
- Given an amount with an exact figure in its tooltip, when I reach it by keyboard (Tab) or touch (tap) instead of the mouse, then the exact figure is shown and is read out with the amount, and Escape closes it (RNF-010, P8-110).
- Given a table on the cost page with amounts that carry an exact figure in a tooltip, when I press Tab, then the amounts of that table take one tab stop, on the first of them or on the one I reached last, and the next Tab leaves them (an amount whose exact figure equals the shown one is plain text and takes none); and when I press ArrowDown or ArrowUp on an amount, then focus moves to the next or previous amount of the table that carries a tooltip, and Home or End moves to the first or last one, without wrapping at the ends (RNF-010, DEC-217, P8-117).
- Given an exact figure that is shown for an amount the pointer rests on without focus, when I press Escape, then the figure closes, and it opens again when the pointer leaves and enters again; and given an amount that has focus, when I press Escape, then the figure stays closed until the focus leaves the amount and returns to it (RNF-010, WCAG 1.4.13, DEC-217, P8-117).
- Given a table on the cost page with amounts that carry an exact figure, when I focus or hover one of them at 1280 px or 360 px, in the light or the dark theme, then the figure opens as the overlay Tooltip of design-system section 5.1: no cell of the table moves, the table, its scroll region and the page keep their size, and the figure is shown whole, inside the viewport and covered by nothing (RNF-010, DEC-220, P8-127).
- Given any money amount on a page other than the cost page (the live run, History, a project's runs, the accounts, the API calls and their summary, the journey, the header), when it is shown, then it is written by the same formatter as on the cost page (four decimals below $1, two from $1, `$0.00` for exactly zero, `<$0.0001` for a non-zero amount below $0.00005), so no page mixes 3 and 6 decimals; an amount that stands alone or is the cell of a table carries the exact figure in a tooltip when it differs from the one shown, as on the cost page (P8-91, EV-94, DEC-54, DEC-217).
- Given a table on a page other than the cost page with amounts that carry a tooltip, when I press Tab, then its amounts take one tab stop, on the first of them or on the one I reached last, and ArrowDown, ArrowUp, Home and End move between them as on the cost page; an amount whose exact figure equals the shown one is plain text and takes none (P8-91, RNF-010).
- Given an amount written inside a sentence (a cap warning, "left of approved", a limit compared with another), when it is shown, then it is rounded by the same rule without a tooltip, and a sentence that compares two limits writes an amount in all its digits when rounding would hide the difference between them: 1.004 reads `$1.004` beside a limit of `$1.00`, the one place that writes three decimals (P8-91).
- Given an amount left of the amount I approved, when it is shown, then it is rounded down, never up, so it never exceeds what is left: 3,965,000 micro-dollars read `$3.96` and 999,960 read `$0.9999`, not `$3.97` and `$1.00` (P8-24, DEC-206, P8-91).
- Given an amount that stands in a link (the cost in the header of a project's page, which links to its cost page) or the cost of a run on the project page that is exactly zero, when it is shown, then the first carries its exact figure as the title of its link and not as a focusable tooltip, so the link is not read twice and takes no second tab stop, and the second reads `No cost`, as the `P8Project` artboard draws it; every other list reads `$0.00` (P8-91, DEC-217).
  Verification: unit tests on the ledger totals; integration test with an interrupted run; web tests of the cost page for the run naming, price table version, money amount, zero, tiny amount and keyboard or touch scenarios (tab stop, arrow keys and Escape included); unit and integration tests on the route for the per-role split of a run; web tests of the breakdown by run for its Kind, role columns, absent roles and Project total; web tests of the money, tooltip, tab stop, sentence, amount left and zero scenarios of the other pages (P8-91).
  Changed 2026-11-03 by card P8-90: the cost page names runs as History does, names the price table version once per table and writes every amount by DEC-54 with the exact figure in a tooltip (scenarios added). Changed 2026-11-03 by DEC-217: exactly zero reads `$0.00` and a non-zero amount below $0.00005 reads `<$0.0001`, with the exact figure in the tooltip (card P8-110); the tooltip opens for keyboard and touch and closes with Escape (RNF-010, card P8-110). Changed 2026-11-04 by card P8-108: each run line of the cost route carries its cost per role (scenario added). Changed 2026-11-04 by card P8-117: the amounts of a table take one tab stop with arrow, Home and End keys between them, and Escape closes a figure the pointer rests on (scenarios added, DEC-217, RNF-010). Changed 2026-11-04 by card P8-109: the breakdown by run shows each run's Kind, one column per role and a Project total row, as the `P8HistoryCost` artboard draws it (scenarios added, DEC-211, DEC-217). Changed 2026-11-04 by card P8-91: every money amount of the other pages follows the same formatter, tooltip and tab stop, and an amount left of the approved amount rounds down (scenarios added, DEC-54, DEC-217).

**RF-429 Keep a share of the cap for verifying and for the summary** - R1 - Should - Source EV-27 (evaluation pass 2), P3-111, P5-18, P5-20, DEC-166, DEC-170 - ADR-007
As a developer I want a run that reaches its token cap during the readers to still verify what they wrote so that its claims can be planned.

- Given a run whose reader role has no token cap of its own, when the readers have spent past 70% of the run token cap and the claims they wrote are few, then they go on reading: the readers have no fixed share of the run token cap (DEC-193), so a run that writes few claims reads every planned area up to the run cap less the summariser's 10%, and is held only by the room check below, the run cap and the scope caps.
- Given a run whose verifier role has no token cap of its own, when the run's spend (the readers' and every other call of the run) and the verifier's next call would pass the run token cap less the summariser's 10%, then that call is refused as a stop of the verifier only (RF-423, `budget.capReached` with `scope: run-tokens`, `role: verifier`, `capSource: reserve`), and the claims it has not judged are left `Unverified` (RF-304), as for a verifier cap of its own; no fixed share of its own stops the verifier while run cap is left (DEC-193). The 10% left is the summariser's, which still merges the duplicate cards and writes the overview of the run, so a run that stopped at a cap has them (DEC-166). If the verifier reaches that line an understand run ends `Completed`, as for a verifier cap of its own, and the number of `Unverified` claims in the run is the signal that it was cut short; a run ends `CapReached` only when the run cap or a scope cap is used up. A verifier cap in the project settings applies as before and no line is added. No cap is ever passed (DEC-112).
- Given a run on the server's keys (or on the user's own key with a daily cap) in which the user-day cap, the own-day cap or the server-month cap has less room left than the run cost cap, when the run starts, then the readers also get a role cost cap of 70% of the smallest room left in those scope caps, measured at run start (the cap minus what the user's UTC day or the server's UTC month has spent, USD). The readers stop at it as at a role cap (RF-423): they end `StoppedByCap`, the claims they wrote are judged with what is left of the scope cap, and the run ends `CapReached`. A run whose run cost cap is the smallest keeps today's behaviour, and a reader cost cap in the project settings replaces this share. On a resume the room is measured again at the resume, and the share is added to what the run's readers spent before it, so a resumed run (RF-006), whether the day has rolled over or the owner has raised a cap, goes on with a new share of the room it now has. No cap is ever passed (DEC-112): the guard still refuses any call over the run, role, day or month cap (Q-62).
- Given an understand run with claims written and not yet judged, when a reader task is about to start or a reader is about to take another turn, then the run checks that the run's spend so far (every role) plus the estimated cost of judging every written claim not yet judged stays within 90% of the run token cap and of the run cost cap (the verifier's room is what is left of the run cap, DEC-193; a verifier cap in the project settings does not shrink it), with tokens counted as for the caps (DEC-168). The estimate is the run's own average verifier spend per judged claim, and 2,000 tokens per claim (priced at what a reader token has cost the run) before the first judgement. When the check fails, no reader task starts, a running reader task ends at its next turn, the run goes on to verification, and it ends `Completed` when every written claim is judged (`CapReached` as before when a cap still binds); the run page and the event log say once that the readers stopped to leave room to judge the claims written. A run with no claim waiting is never stopped by this check, and a resume counts the claims written before the stop (DEC-170, P5-20).
- Given a run whose spend leaves no room for one more reader call (its output limit) below 90% of the run token cap, or whose readers have no room for one under a reader token cap of the project settings, when the next reader task is about to start or a reader is about to take another turn, then no reader task starts and no call is made to be refused; the run goes on to verification and ends `CapReached`, as the run cap less the summariser's part is used up, and the run page and the event log say so once (`budget.readersStopped` with the reason `share`) (P5-20, DEC-193).
- Given an understand run or a Deepen with several parallel readers, when a reader is about to make a call, then the room check counts the calls in flight as the budget guard counts their reservations (RF-422): the call is made when the run token and cost caps can pay for its worst case beside what is spent and the worst cases of the calls in flight, and otherwise it waits until one of them has ended and been booked, and asks again. At four readers and a run cap under two worst-case calls the first call is therefore answered and booked, not cancelled by the refusal of a second one, and the run ends as one reader would end it (`CapReached` after the spend booked, the area `Partly read`) (Q-67, P8-132). With no call in flight the call goes to the budget guard, which refuses what the caps cannot pay for as RF-422 says: a cap that cannot pay for one call at its worst case reads no area, the call is not made, no spend is booked and the run ends `CapReached`. A run at a normal cap, whose caps pay for the calls of every reader, starts every reader at once, and a run of one reader is not changed.
- Given a Deepen run with claims written in its rounds and not yet judged, when a reader task of a round is about to start or a reader is about to take another turn, then the same check runs over the claims of the whole Deepen (every round, and the rounds of a resumed Deepen); when it fails no further reader task starts, the round goes on to judge every claim written, the run says so once with `budget.readersStopped`, and the Deepen ends `Completed` with the stop reason `readersStopped` (a Deepen whose readers have no room for one more call, as in the scenario above, ends `CapReached` as before) (P5-30). A Deepen whose verifier reaches its line of the run cap ends as an understand run does: the claims it did not judge are `Unverified` and the line alone does not end it `CapReached`; a verifier cap of the project settings still does (DEC-193).
- Given a reader token cap in the project settings, when the readers reach it, then that cap applies as before (RF-423, RF-304); no share of the run token cap is added either way (DEC-193), so the first reader calls of a model that fits the default cost cap are still let through (Q-57).
- Given the start page for an understand run on local models only, when the likely tokens of the chosen selection are above the run token cap, then the page names both figures and asks to confirm before it starts (RF-425).
  Verification: unit tests of the caps and the start page; integration tests with the fake provider where the readers reach their share of a scope cap (the user-day cap, the server-month cap) or the run cap less the summariser's part, a run with few claims that reads past 70% of the run cap, and the verifier judges the claim; a claim-heavy recorded run in which the readers stop and every written claim is judged, and a resume that counts the claims written before the stop (P5-20); the same for a Deepen, with the stop reason `readersStopped` (P5-30).
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-193: the verifier's room is what is left of the run token cap after the readers' spend, minus the summariser's 10%, not a fixed 20% share; the readers have no fixed share either and stop only at the 90% line (their next call would pass the run token cap less the summariser's 10%), at a reader cap of the project settings, or when the run cap left can no longer hold the judging of the claims waiting; and a run ends `CapReached` only when the run cap or a scope cap is used up. In this requirement the run cap is used up when the run token cap less the summariser's 10% has no room for the next call (card P7-01). Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-194: a run whose readers stopped with planned areas unread ends `CapReached` and is shown as "Partly read", never `Completed` (card P7-02).

**RF-430 A likely and a worst-case total before the run** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-196, DEC-206, EV-62, EV-84 - ADR-007, ADR-026
As a user I want to see what the whole journey is likely to cost, and what it may cost at worst, so that the amount I approve is realistic.

- Given a code map and settings, when the understand estimate is shown, then it gives a likely and a worst-case total for the journey: understand with claim checking, the default Deepen rounds and the plan, in tokens and, for priced roles, dollars.
- Given earlier runs of the same provider and model in the cost ledger (RF-428), when the estimate is made, then it scales their measured totals by the source size; with no history it uses the fixed assumptions and says so.
- Given the estimate, when I start the run, then the start button shows the figure I approve and the run records it as its approved amount (RF-432).
- Given the journey estimate, when it is shown, then it gives the likely figure and, beside it, the figure I approve: the lower of the worst case and the administrator ceilings that bind the run (the run cap and the user's day cap on server keys, my own day cap on my key, and the run ceilings of 50,000,000 tokens and $100); no estimate shows an approvable figure above those ceilings, and it says when a ceiling lowered it (DEC-206).
- Given two estimates of the same project whose basis differs (RST-2310), when the second is shown, then it says why the figure moved: the first used the fixed assumptions and the second the measured runs of this model, or the other way round (DEC-206).
- Given the estimate whose likely figure, in tokens or in dollars, is above the approvable figure, when any line of the start page names the likely figure (the journey block and the bar at the foot of the page), then that line says what follows: the run pauses and asks at the approved amount (P9-11, EV-96).
- Given an estimate the start page showed, when a later read on the same page gives another approvable figure (the measured runs replaced the fixed assumptions, or a ceiling, a setting or a key changed), then the page does not replace the figure silently: it says "The approvable figure moved from $1.32 to $0.3140" with the reason, and Analyse names and approves the figure now shown; a figure the earlier read showed is never approved, because the request that confirms it is refused with RST-2310 (P9-11, EV-96).
  Verification: unit tests of the estimator with and without history; an end-to-end test of the start button.
  Applied 2026-11-01 by P7-04: the history is the ledger rows of the project's understand and plan runs (a Deepen is an understand run) on the reader's provider and model, summed and scaled by the source size; the likely total is that sum and the worst case half as much again, never below the understand run's high figure. Without history a Deepen round counts as 50% (likely) and 100% (worst case) of the understand estimate and the plan as 10% and 20%, with the default rounds (DEC-204 keeps the figure for the journey's approval screen). The approved figure is the likely total; the run records it in its manifest options as `approvedAmount` (data-model.md section 3.6). Changed 2026-11-01 by DEC-205: the approved figure is the worst-case total; the start button states both and says that starting approves the worst case.
  Changed 2026-11-02 by DEC-206 (P8-03): the approved figure is the lower of the worst-case total and the administrator ceilings, not the worst case alone; the estimate states the likely figure and that lower figure, and the start button says that starting approves it (EV-84: a worst case of $314.17 stood against a $4 run cap, so the pause never triggered).
  Changed 2026-11-01 by DEC-204: the start button of this requirement is the Analyse button of the journey (RF-434), which approves the worst-case total of the whole journey once; later steps do not ask again (RF-435).

**RF-431 No run without a usable key** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-196, EV-44 - ADR-007, ADR-015
As a user I want to know before I start that a provider key is missing so that a run is never queued only to fail at $0.

- Given a role whose provider needs a key and neither my key nor an allowed server key is set, when the understand estimate is shown, then that role says the key is missing.
- Given such a role, when I start an understand run, a Deepen or a resume, then it is refused with RST-2302 and a plain message before it is queued, and no run is recorded.
  Verification: integration tests of the start routes; a unit test of the estimate.
  Applied 2026-11-01 by P7-04: a plan run's planner is checked the same way; an empty server key variable (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=`) counts as no key (EV-44).

**RF-432 Pause and ask near the approved amount** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-196 - ADR-007, ADR-026
As a user I want a run that nears the amount I approved to pause and ask me so that I do not lose the run at a hard cap.

- Given a running run whose spend plus the room of its next call would pass its approved amount, when the budget guard checks the call, then the run writes its work, moves to the queue state `AwaitingApproval` ("Waiting for your approval"), releases its running slot and asks "Continue (+N)" or "Stop here".
- Given a paused run, when I choose Continue, then its approved amount grows by N and it waits in the queue like a resumed run (RF-006); when I choose Stop, then it ends `CapReached` with the same saved result as a run that reached its cap.
- Given a paused run, when I choose Stop here, then it ends `CapReached` with the end reason `StoppedAtApproval` and no error code, and every page words it as my stop at the approved amount ("Stopped at the approved amount"), never as a cap that was reached and never with RST-1600 ("Raise the run cap"); its Resume line says that Resume goes on with the run and asks for a new approval before it spends past that amount, and does not ask to raise a cap; a run that a real cap stopped (a hard ceiling, a day, month, server or role cap, or a pause that could not be offered) keeps the end reason none, its code RST-1600 and the wording of a cap (P9-03, EV-101).
- Given a run stopped at the approved amount that left areas unread, when the strip shows its step, then the detail still reads "Partly read" (without an error code, which this stop has none of); and given such a run that is resumed and reaches the approved amount again, then it pauses again at `AwaitingApproval` with no end reason and asks again (P9-03, P9-14).
- Given a paused run, when the server restarts, then it is still `AwaitingApproval`; only its owner can answer, and another user's run answers 404.
- Given the user-day, own-day, server-month or a role cap, when it binds, then it stays a hard limit and the run ends `CapReached` as before (DEC-112).
- Given a paused run that is a step of a journey (RF-011, RF-435), when I choose Continue (+N), then the journey's approved amount grows by the same N when the Continue is accepted, so what is left of the journey is what was left before plus N; neither amount grows without the other (a Continue that cannot queue the run raises neither); the journey's amount stays within the administrator ceilings of the run caps (50,000,000 tokens, $100), and where they bind, N is what the journey can still take and the run takes no more: the pause offers only that N, and where the journey can take none of N (in tokens, or in dollars where N has dollars) Continue could add nothing, so the run does not pause and ends `CapReached` as at a hard ceiling (DEC-206), and a Continue the journey can take none of (another step took the room first) is refused with RST-2311 and raises nothing; a run that is not a step of a journey (a project with no journey, a run started by the evaluation tools, a run queued before the current journey) grows alone, as before, and a run queued before the current journey keeps its queue time when continued, so it never becomes a step of that journey (P9-01, EV-100).
- Given a paused step of a journey whose room shrank after the pause (another step took some of it), when I choose Continue (+N), then the run takes only the N the journey can still take and the answer (202) says so: it names the N the run got (`granted`) and `lessThanOffered` is true when that is less than the N the pause offered, in tokens or in dollars; where the room did not shrink `granted` is the N offered and `lessThanOffered` is false (P9-14, EV-100).
- Given a paused run that was queued before the current journey and is continued, when it waits in the queue, then it keeps its old queue time (above), so it goes ahead of every run queued since, other users' runs included, as RF-1108 orders the queue by queue time (RF-1108, P9-14).
- Given a paused `plan` run, when I choose Continue, then it goes on with the planner answers it already paid for (kept in its run folder, `planner-answers.json`, with the paid part of a cut-off answer) and makes only the calls it had not made, numbering its calls on from the earlier attempt; when the brief, an answer, a card or a setting changed during the pause, it uses none of the kept answers, says so in its log and asks the planner again; the run page shows the pause and the question as for understand (P8-01, EV-82).
  Verification: integration tests with the fake provider for Continue and Stop; a queue test of the released slot; an end-to-end test of the question.
  Changed 2026-11-02 by DEC-206 (P8-03): the amount set at the start is the lower of the server's worst-case total and the administrator ceilings, and the figure the start button sent must match that lower figure within 2% (RST-2310); where the approved line equals the administrator's run cap, or is not under the user's day cap, that cap stays the hard limit (the run ends `CapReached` there), because Continue could add nothing.
  Changed 2026-11-02 by P8-03: the readers' room check (RF-429), which stops the readers before a call that would pass the run cap, pauses the run and asks only when the cap that binds it, in tokens and in dollars, is the approved amount (an administrator ceiling that binds, such as a run cap lowered after the approval, stops the readers and ends the run as before); before, it ended the run `CapReached` without asking (found by the end-to-end pause test, tests/e2e/journeys/approval-pause.spec.ts).
  Applied 2026-11-01 by P7-05: the approved amount replaces the project's run caps as the run's line (a run without one keeps the hard stop at its project caps); a call that would pass it is refused before it is made, the run's tasks stop and save their work as at a cap, and the run moves to `AwaitingApproval` and emits `run.awaitingApproval`. The server sets the approved amount from its own journey estimate at the start: its worst-case total (DEC-205); the figure the start button sent is only a confirmation and must match that worst case within 2% (else RST-2310, 409). N is half the approved amount; Continue never adds more than that N, and no approved amount passes the administrator ceilings of the run caps (50,000,000 tokens, $100). Continue is `POST /api/runs/:runId/continue`; Stop here is `POST /api/runs/:runId/stop`, which ends a paused run `CapReached` with RST-1600 and its unread areas. A pause that cannot be recorded ends the run `CapReached` (fail closed). The administrator's run cap stays a hard limit with the day, month and role caps.

**RF-433 No cap for local models** - R1 - Could - Source DEC-197, EV-62 - ADR-007, ADR-026
As a user of a local model I want a run that costs nothing to go on without a token cap so that it is not stopped for tokens I do not pay for.

- Given the project setting "No cap for local models" on (it is off by default) and a run whose every role is on a local provider, when the run reaches its token cap, then it goes on without pausing; every call is still metered.
- Given the setting on and a run whose every role is on a local provider, when the run reaches the amount its owner approved, then it does not pause (RF-432); the administrator ceilings of the run caps (50,000,000 tokens, $100) and the day, month and role caps stay hard limits (DEC-112), so such a run still ends `CapReached` at the ceiling.
- Given the setting off (the default), when a run whose every role is on a local provider reaches its token cap or its approved amount, then it stops or pauses as any run does (RF-422, RF-432).
- Given a run with one role on a cloud provider, when it runs, then every cap and the pause of RF-432 apply, whatever the setting.
- Given a provider of kind `ollama` whose server is not this machine (the host of `ROSETTA_OLLAMA_URL` is not a loopback name or address or `host.docker.internal`, RF-143), when a run uses it, then it is not a local provider: it is priced and capped like a cloud provider, the setting does not lift its caps, and a model without a price is refused with RST-1601 and a plain message (RF-427).
- Given the setting on and a run whose every role is on a local provider, when the run starts, when its Deepen is estimated and when its plan caps are shown, then each shows the administrator ceiling of the run caps (50,000,000 tokens, $100) as the run cap, not the project's, and the start event names the administrator as its source; the three agree with the guard.
- Given the setting on and a run whose every role is local, when its reported usage reaches the administrator ceiling, then the run ends `CapReached` there.
- Given the project settings page, when I tick "No cap for local models" and save, then the project stores `budget.noCapForLocalModels: true`; a project that never set it reads as off.
  Verification: integration tests with the fake provider (a local kind), with the setting on and off; a unit test of the cloud role; a web test of the settings field.
  Applied 2026-11-01 by P7-06: a local provider is one of the kinds the price table prices at zero (`ollama`, `fake`); DEC-197 does not lift the administrator ceilings, so they stay.
  Applied 2026-11-02 by P8-04: an `ollama` provider is local only when the host of `ROSETTA_OLLAMA_URL` is this machine (the rule of RF-143); on a remote host the price lookup no longer gives the zero price, so the call is priced like a cloud call or refused as unpriced (RST-1601). The run start event, the Deepen estimate and the plan caps take the run caps from `runCapsFor`, the same function the budget guard uses. Verification adds unit tests of the three displays and the remote host, and an integration test that runs an all-local run to `CapReached` at the ceiling.

**RF-434 One estimate and one Analyse button for the journey** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-196, DEC-204, DEC-205, DEC-206 - ADR-026, ADR-027
As a user I want one estimate for the whole journey and one button so that I approve the spend once.

- Given a scanned project, when the project page opens, then it shows one estimate for the journey (understand, claim checking, one Deepen round, the plan) with a likely and a worst-case figure in tokens and, for priced roles, dollars (RF-430), and one button, Analyse.
- Given the estimate, when I press Analyse, then the journey records the approvable figure, the lower of the worst-case total and the administrator ceilings, as its approved amount (DEC-206, which replaces DEC-205's unbounded worst case; the request confirms the shown figure within 2%, RST-2310) and the understand run starts; the button says that pressing it approves that figure, and the start page states the likely figure beside it.
- Given a project that already has a journey, when I press Analyse again, then the page says that this starts a new approval: the earlier amount no longer applies and the journey's spend counts from zero (P8-03).
- Given the estimate whose likely figure is above the approvable figure (DEC-210, Q-65), when it is shown, then it shows the approvable figure with "likely above what can be approved (likely $4.09)" and says that the run will pause and ask at the approved amount; the approved amount of a journey does not change between two reads of the estimate with the same inputs (P8-24, EV-96).
- Given a project with a journey, when its start page shows the estimate, then the one limit the page names for the run is the journey's approved amount: the likely figure is stated against that amount, and the project run cap and the link to change it are shown only when the project has no journey, because the approved amount replaces the project's run caps (RF-435, EV-85; P9-12).
- Given a project with a journey, when the Deepen panel of one of its runs shows its estimate, then it shows three figures, the first round, up to N rounds and what stops it, where what stops it is what is left of the approved amount ("$3.96 left of $4.00"), the high-figure warning is shown once and against that amount, and the question before the start is one line (the likely cost and the amount left) that does not repeat the panel; a Deepen of a project without a journey names its run cap instead (P8-24, UX-28).
- Given the estimate whose likely figure is above the approvable figure, when the bar at the foot of the start page shows the likely figure, then it adds that the run pauses and asks at the approved amount, so no line names a likely figure above the approvable one without saying what follows (P9-11).
- Given a journey that has an approved amount, when a later read of the estimate on the same page gives another approvable figure, then the page keeps showing which figure the first read gave and says that it moved and why; it never swaps the figure without a word (P9-11, EV-96).
- Given a role without a usable key (RF-431), when the estimate is shown, then it says so and Analyse is refused with RST-2302 before any run is queued.
- Given a project with a journey, when its start page shows the estimate, then no warning names the project's run caps, the server run cap note is not shown and no link leads to the project's run caps (a role's cap warning keeps its "Review the caps" link), because the approved amount replaces the project's run caps and is the one limit the page names (the administrator ceilings are already inside the approvable figure); the caps that still bind a step (a role's cap, the day and month caps) are warned about as before, and a project with no journey keeps the run cap warning, the note and the link (RF-434, RF-435, RF-1106; P9-14).
- Given an approvable figure that moved between two reads of the start page, when the note says so, then the note is a status the screen reader announces, and the first read the page showed stays the one the later reads are compared with, also when a read in between (the areas changed) was loading (P9-11, P9-14).
- Given the start page of a project whose roles that an understand run calls (the reader, the verifier and the summariser; the planner is called by the plan alone) each use a provider the page knows cannot answer now (an Ollama that answered it could not be reached, or a provider with no key the user can use; the rule of the role warning, RF-436), and at least one of them an unreachable Ollama, when the estimate is shown, then Analyse is disabled with the reason beside it ("Every role uses a provider that cannot answer now, so a run would fail at its first call. Fix the roles above first."), the reason is also the button's title, like every disabled reason of the page, and nothing is queued; while the answer on Ollama has not come or could not be had, Analyse is not held back (P9-09, EV-103, P9-14).
- Given some of the roles an understand run calls use a provider the page knows cannot answer and others one it can use, when the estimate is shown, then Analyse stays available and the role warning (RF-436) names those roles (P9-09).
- Given the start page and an Ollama that answered it could not be reached, when the private-code notice would offer "Switch every role to Ollama", then the button is not shown, as a switch to a provider that cannot answer cannot work; it shows once Ollama answers with its models, and while the answer has not come it shows as before (P9-09, EV-103).
  Verification: unit tests of the journey estimate; integration tests of the start request; an end-to-end test of the button.

**RF-435 Every step runs under the one approved amount** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-196, DEC-204, DEC-205, DEC-206 - ADR-026, ADR-027
As a user I want every step to run under the amount I approved and ask me near it so that no step asks for caps again.

- Given a journey with an approved amount, when any of its steps runs, then its budget line is the journey's remaining amount, and a call that would pass the journey's spend is refused before it is made.
- Given a step that nears the amount, when the budget guard checks the call, then the run pauses and asks "Continue (+N)" or "Stop here" (RF-432); the approved amount (the lower of the worst-case journey total and the administrator ceilings, DEC-206) is the pause line, and the day, month, server and role caps stay hard limits (DEC-112).
- Given a step that starts, when it starts, then it does not ask for caps, a stage or an amount again.
- Given two steps of one journey queued or running together, when the second starts, then its line is what is left of the approved amount after the spend of the journey and after what the first step may still use of its own line (its reservation), so the lines of the steps never add up to more than the approved amount (P8-03); the line is read and used by one start at a time for each project, so two starts never read the same free line.
- Given a journey whose approved amount is used up (nothing left in tokens, or in dollars where the amount has dollars), when a step is started, then it is refused with RST-2311 and a plain message before any run is queued, and no run is recorded (P8-03).
- Given the start page and the project settings, when they describe the caps, then one sentence says which cap applies to a journey step: the amount approved on Analyse replaces the project run cap, the administrator's caps and the day and month caps stay hard limits; no other sentence on the page says the run stops before any call that could pass the cap (EV-85).
- Given a step of the journey that paused and was continued with N (RF-432), when the journey is read or a later step starts, then the journey's approved amount includes N and its remainder is the remainder before the Continue plus N, so Plan setup's options and Start the plan run under it without a new Analyse and are not refused with RST-2311 while that remainder is above zero (P9-01, EV-100).
- Given a step of an earlier journey that ended at a cap, failed or was interrupted, when I press Resume (RF-006) after Analyse recorded a newer journey, then the run keeps its queue time and stays a step of the journey it was queued under, so its spend never moves into the newer journey and the newer journey's remainder is what it was before the Resume (P9-16, as for Continue, RF-432); a step of the current journey, and a run of a project with no journey, is queued at the end of the queue as before.
- Given a journey whose approved amount is used up (RST-2311 above), when Plan setup asks the planner for its options, then the call is refused with RST-2311, not RST-1600, before any model call, and a call that the journey's remainder stops mid-way answers RST-2311 too; RST-1600 keeps its words for a project with no journey. RST-2311 names the one way on the product has: press Analyse again on the start page to approve a new amount (no other action approves a new journey amount without a new understand run; Continue at an approval pause raises it only for a paused step, RF-432). The same words serve a start refused for lack of room and a Continue the journey can take none of (P9-02, EV-100).
- Given a project with a journey, when a `plan` run starts (or Plan setup asks the planner for its options), then it takes what is left of the approved amount (recorded in the run's manifest) as its line in place of the project run caps, pauses there (RF-432) and never ends `CapReached` at the project run cap while the journey amount allows the call; a project with no journey (a run started by the evaluation tools) keeps its project run caps as a hard stop (P8-01, EV-82).
  Verification: integration tests with the fake provider across two chained steps, for the shared line, the pause and Continue; a test that no start request carries caps.

**RF-436 Caps, models and parallelism live in project settings** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-117, DEC-204 - ADR-007, ADR-027
As a user I want my caps, models and parallelism in my project settings so that they apply to the whole journey without being asked at each step.

- Given the project settings, when I open them, then they hold the run caps, the models per role and the parallelism that the start page held before (RF-420..RF-429), and the journey uses them for every step; the approved amount (RF-434) is the per-journey limit.
- Given the run-start API, when a caller sends a stage (the evaluation tools and the administrator do), then it starts that single stage under the same checks (RF-430..RF-432, the egress guard, ownership); the web pages never send a stage or caps.
- Given a project whose reader is a local model (Ollama) with `agents.parallelism` 1, when the settings are saved with the reader on a hosted provider and the value is still the 1 that was stored, then it is set to the template default of 4 and the answer says so; any other value, and a value changed in the same save, is kept; switching from a hosted provider to a local one changes nothing; the help text of "Parallel readers" states this rule (P8-19).
- Given a save where that rule fired, when the answer comes back, then the form shows the stored value (4), "Settings saved." stays, and a second line under it says why ("Parallel readers set from 1 to 4, because the reader is no longer a local model."); a save where the rule did not fire shows "Settings saved." alone; any edit after the save, even one that puts the saved values back, hides both for good until the next save (P8-57).
- Given the project settings and a role whose provider I change to one with a shipped model list (Anthropic or OpenAI, `prices/models.yaml`, RF-405), when the list is shown, then the role's model is the first model of that list, in the order of the file, that the role can use (for the reader, the first one not marked as unable to call tools), never "Choose a model"; the other three roles keep their provider and model (P8-72).
- Given the list of that provider has not arrived when I change the provider, when it arrives, then the model is set the same way, unless I have typed or chosen a model for that role in the meantime; a list that cannot be loaded leaves the plain text field and an empty model as before (P8-72).
- Given the shipped model list (`prices/models.yaml`), when it is read, then the recommended model of a provider is the first usable one in its order, and the list leads with a low-cost model: Claude Haiku 5.5 for Anthropic and `gpt-5-nano` for OpenAI, never Claude Opus 5.5; the owner confirmed this order (Q-68, DEC-223) (P8-72).
- Given a role whose provider list is shown, when I open the model choice, then the model that the rule above picks for that role carries "(recommended)" after its name, and the Roles section says "Choosing a provider selects its recommended model; change it if you want another."; a model I choose instead stays, and a project opened with saved models shows them unchanged (P8-72).
- Given a provider with no shipped list (Ollama, whose models are the ones installed, and OpenAI-compatible), when I change a role to it, then the model stays empty as before ("Choose a model" or the text field), and no model is marked "(recommended)" (P8-72).
- Given the project settings, when the "Roles" section is shown (named "Models per role" before P8-135), then its first row is the every-role control of the `P8ProjectSettings` artboard: "Provider for every role", "Model" and the button "Apply to every role"; the provider list holds only the providers I can use now: one I saved a key for, one whose server key I may use (the provider's `keySource` is `user-then-server`, the administrator allows server keys for my account and the server holds a key), and Ollama when it answered with its models; a provider with no key, an Ollama that could not be reached and the fake of the tests are not listed, and with no provider left the row is not shown (DEC-225, P8-73).
- Given the every-role control with a provider chosen, when I open "Model", then its first option is "The recommended one per role", followed by the models of that provider's list; when I press "Apply to every role", then the four roles take that provider and each role takes the model that choosing the provider for that role alone selects (the rule above, so the reader skips a model that cannot call tools and Ollama leaves the model empty), or the model I chose for all four; the other settings are unchanged (DEC-225, P8-73).
- Given I pressed "Apply to every role", when nothing else happens, then nothing is saved: the roles change only in the form, "Save settings" saves them, and the edit hides "Settings saved." and its second line like any other edit (DEC-225, P8-73).
- Given a signed-in user, when the settings page asks `GET /api/account/key-access`, then the answer lists each provider that takes a key (`anthropic`, `openai`, `openai-compatible`) with two booleans: `ownKey` (the user saved a key for it) and `serverKey` (the administrator allows the account server keys and the server holds one); no key, no part of a key and nothing about another user is in it; without a session the answer is 401, and an account that must change its password is refused as on the other account routes (DEC-225, P8-73).
- Given the project settings and a role whose provider I cannot use now (an Ollama that answered that it could not be reached, or a provider that takes a key for which I have neither a key of my own nor a server key I may use, by the rule of the every-role control), when the page has the answers on Ollama and on my keys, then a warning at the top of the page, above "Roles", says so for each such provider, with the roles that use it: "Ollama cannot be reached. All four roles use it, so a run would fail at its first call." or "OpenAI has no key you can use. The planner uses it, so a run would be refused (RST-2302)." ("The reader and the verifier use it" for two roles); no role is changed by it, and a new project keeps the roles of the administrator's `defaultRoles` (DEC-106) (P8-74).
- Given that warning and a provider I can use, when it is shown, then it ends with what I can use, named after the provider the every-role control has chosen first: "You have an Anthropic key.", "You may use the server's Anthropic key." or "Ollama can be reached.", and it holds the every-role control (DEC-225), whose provider list also shows an Ollama that could not be reached as "Ollama (not reachable)", which cannot be chosen; while the warning is shown the control is not repeated at the top of "Roles". With no provider I can use, it ends "Add your own key in Settings and keys, or ask the administrator to allow the server's key." and holds no control (P8-74).
- Given the warning, when I press "Apply to every role" in it, then the roles change in the form as the every-role rule above says and nothing is saved until "Save settings"; the warning goes, as every role can now be used, and focus moves to "Provider for every role" at the top of "Roles" (P8-74).
- Given every role uses a provider I can use, or the answer on Ollama or on my keys has not come or could not be had, when the page is shown, then there is no warning (P8-74).
- Given the start page of a project (before and after its first run) and a role whose provider I cannot use now, by the rule of the settings-page warning above, when the page has the answers on Ollama and on my keys, then the same warning is shown at the top of the page, under the project's title, with the same words, the same ending and the same every-role control; the start page holds no roles of its own (the run takes them from the project settings, DEC-204), so the warning also says "Apply to every role saves this choice in the project settings, so every run of this project uses it."; the warning itself changes no role, and a new project keeps the roles of the administrator's `defaultRoles` (DEC-106) (P8-75).
- Given that warning on the start page, when I press "Apply to every role", then the project settings are read again and saved with the four roles on that provider, each with the model the every-role rule gives it (a model chosen in the control for all four, else the recommended one per role; with Ollama and no model chosen, a role already on Ollama keeps its model and the others take the default local model, as the switch of RF-132 does); every other setting is kept; the warning goes, the estimate is asked again, and a line at the top, which takes the focus, says what was saved: "Saved in the project settings: every role now uses Anthropic.", followed by the parallel-readers line of the settings page when that rule fired; a refused save changes nothing and shows its error under the warning (P8-75).
- Given every role uses a provider I can use, or the answer on Ollama or on my keys has not come or could not be had, when the start page is shown, then there is no warning (P8-75).
- Given a role whose output limit the project does not set (Q-57), when the project settings are shown, then its "max output tokens" field and its slider show the same number, the limit the role runs with (RF-405: on Anthropic or OpenAI the smaller of the role's hosted default and the model's maximum in the shipped list, on any other provider the role's local default), and the help under the field says "8,192 is the default. The field and the slider always show the same value."; the field never reads "Not set" or stays empty next to a slider at a value. Saving without changing it still leaves the limit out of the document, so the role keeps following its model; a number typed or a slider moved is stored, and a field emptied by hand shows the default again when it loses focus and is saved as not set (DEC-106, P8-76).
- Given a number field of the project settings whose value can run to millions ("Run token cap", "Largest file to read (bytes)", "Area token budget"), when it holds a number, then the number is also written with thousands separators beside the field ("2,000,000 tokens", "1,000,000 bytes"), and every number in the settings' labels and help text is written with them; the field itself keeps the digits as typed, so a number is typed without separators and saved as before (P8-76).
- Given the project settings, when the "Roles" section is shown, then it is laid out as the Roles table of the `P8ProjectSettings` artboard (DEC-211, DEC-224): the heading "Roles" with "Choosing a provider selects its recommended model; change it if you want another." beside it, the every-role control (or, while the warning is shown, nothing in its place), then one table named "Roles" with the columns Role, Provider, Model and State and one row per role in the order Reader, Verifier, Planner, Summariser; each row names its role beside a dot in the role's colour, and its provider and model are chosen in the row, with the same labels as before ("Reader provider", "Reader model", "Reader other model"); the output limits, the reader's turn limit and the Ollama context stay in the section under the table (P8-135).
- Given a row of the Roles table, when the page has the answer on Ollama or on my keys that the role's provider needs, then its State says whether that provider can be used now, by the rule of the warning above: "Key ready" with a check for a provider that takes a key for which I have my own key or a server key I may use, "No key" with a cross for one without, "Reachable" with a check for an Ollama that answered with its models, and "Not reachable" with a cross for an Ollama that could not be reached; before that answer has come, when it could not be had, and for a provider that is neither Ollama nor one that takes a key, the State is empty; changing the provider in the row changes its State at once, before any save (P8-135).
- Given the Roles table, when it is shown at the design system's widths (1280 px and wider, below 1280 px, and a phone at 360 px), in light and in dark, then nothing of the page lies beside the view: from 640 px the four columns stay, and below 640 px each row stacks as the design system's data table does, the role first and each other cell under its column name; the dot and the State take the theme's role and status colours (P8-135).
  Verification: web tests of the settings fields; an integration test that an evaluation run still starts a single stage through the API; unit tests of the provider-switch rule; web tests of the page line (shown with the stored value, absent when the rule did not fire, hidden by any edit including a revert) and an end-to-end check of the line (P8-57); web tests of the model chosen with the provider: set at once from a loaded list, set when the list arrives, not set over a model the user chose, reader skipping a model that cannot call tools, the other roles unchanged, no model for Ollama, and the shipped list leading with a low-cost model per provider (P8-72); web tests of the every-role control (only usable providers listed, the row absent with none, the model choice, Apply filling the four roles, nothing saved until Save, keyboard use) and an integration test of `GET /api/account/key-access` (own key, server key allowed or not, another user's key not counted, no key material, 401 without a session) (P8-73); unit tests of the warning rule (Ollama unreachable, no key, every role usable, answers not yet known) and web tests of the warning (the providers and roles named, the control inside it with the unreachable Ollama not choosable, one Apply giving Anthropic with its recommended models and the focus moved, no warning with every role usable) (P8-74). Web tests of the start-page warning (Ollama unreachable, no key, every role usable, Apply saving the project settings and moving the focus to the saved line) (P8-75). Web tests of the number fields: an unset output limit shown in the field and the slider at the built-in default of its provider and model with the default line, saved as not set, stored when typed, back to the default when emptied; the built-in default equal to the one runs use (role-limits); the grouped value beside the large-number fields (P8-76). Unit tests of the State of a role (each state, empty while an answer is missing) and web tests of the Roles table (its columns and row order, the labels kept, each State shown and changed with the provider, the every-role control above it), and the visual sweep of the settings page in its four views asserting the table (P8-135).

### 5.6 Report (RF-500..RF-599)

**RF-500 HTML report** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-05 - ADR-005
As a developer I want an HTML report of a run so that I and others can explore the findings.

- Given a finished run, when I press Build report on it, then a static site is written with a dashboard (cards by type, claims by status, coverage, cost), a page per area, a page per card, the open questions and the cost report.
  Verification: snapshot tests of generated pages; manual check in a browser.

**RF-501 Self-contained and publishable** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-04 - ADR-002
As a developer I want the report to work from the file system and on GitHub Pages so that I can share it without a server.

- Given the generated report, when it is opened from disk or served from a sub-path, then every page, style and link works with no external network request.
  Verification: end-to-end test with a headless browser from disk and from a sub-path.

**RF-502 Show the evidence** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-19 - ADR-005
As a reader I want each claim's cited lines shown next to it so that I can check it in seconds.

- Given a claim with citations, when its card page is shown, then each citation displays the cited lines with a few lines of context, line numbers, and the claim status and reason.
  Verification: snapshot test.

**RF-503 Filter findings** - R1 - Should - Source interview 2026-10-09 - ADR-002
As a reader I want to filter cards by type, area and status so that I can focus.

- Given the cards list, when a filter is chosen, then only matching cards are shown, without a server.
  Verification: end-to-end test.

**RF-505 Download everything from the report** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-24 - ADR-009
As a reader of a published report I want a "Download all" link so that I get every card, the specification and the
folder structure without cloning anything.

- Given a generated report, when it is written, then it includes a zip of the run (RF-008) and, when a hand-off package exists, a second zip of it, linked from the dashboard; the links work from disk and on GitHub Pages.
  Verification: end-to-end test that downloads and unzips both files.

**RF-506 Replay the agents in the report** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-46 - ADR-012
As a reader of a published report I want to watch how the agents worked so that the analysis is understandable and convincing.

- Given a run with `events.jsonl`, when its report is generated, then a "Run replay" page shows the agents spawned, their timeline of turns and tool calls, the cards they produced, the verifier's verdicts and the cost over time, with play, pause and step controls; without JavaScript it shows the same timeline as a static list.
  Verification: end-to-end test with a headless browser on a recorded run; accessibility check.

**RF-507 Render repository and model text safely** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-57 - ADR-013
As a reader I want the report and the web page unable to run anything that came from the analysed code or a model so that opening them is always safe.

- Given card text, claims, reasons, code excerpts or file names, when they are rendered in the web UI or the report, then they are rendered as text; raw HTML in Markdown is not rendered; `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` and `innerHTML` are banned by a lint rule.
- Given a link inside model text, when it is rendered, then only `https://github.com/` links stay links; any other scheme or host is shown as plain text.
- Given a fixture card containing `<script>`, an `onerror` attribute and a `javascript:` link, when its page is opened, then nothing runs (checked in the browser test).
  Verification: end-to-end test with the fixture card; lint rule test.

**RF-504 Markdown report for the milestone** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-04, DEC-18, DEC-131 - ADR-005
As a developer I want a Markdown report before the HTML one exists so that the milestone has a readable output.

- Given a finished run, when the Markdown report is built on it, then `report.md` links the specification, every card, the coverage and the cost report.
- Given a run that has ended, when its owner asks for the report (`POST /api/runs/:runId/report`), then `report.md`, the static `report.html` and the replay page (RF-506) are written to the run's `report/` folder, rendered as RF-507 requires, and the answer lists the files written (DEC-131).
- Given a run that is queued or running, when its owner asks for the report, then the answer is 409 `runInProgress` and nothing is written (DEC-131).
- Given another user's run or an unknown run id, when the report is asked for, then the answer is the same 404 as for any missing run, so the run's existence is not revealed (DEC-131).
  Verification: snapshot test.

**RF-508 Diagrams in the report** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-134 - ADR-013
As a reader I want the flows, screen map and data model drawn in the HTML report so that I do not need another tool to see them.

- Given Mermaid diagrams in the specification or the package, when the HTML report is built, then each is drawn by a pinned copy of Mermaid shipped with the report (no network), with `securityLevel: 'strict'`, and the Markdown keeps the Mermaid source.
- Given a diagram whose labels hold text from the analysed code, when it is generated, then every label is escaped by the generator so that it cannot close the diagram, add a link or a click handler, or inject HTML; RF-507 applies to the drawn diagram.
- Given a diagram that fails to draw, when the page is opened, then its source is shown as text with a note, and the rest of the page works.
  Verification: end-to-end test with a fixture whose names hold Mermaid syntax, HTML and a `javascript:` link; nothing runs.

### 5.7 Plan: the hand-off package (RF-600..RF-699)

**RF-600 Produce the hand-off package** - Withdrawn 2026-10-11 - see RF-604 (DEC-59: no command-line interface; DEC-77, DEC-78: the planning brief replaces `--target`)

**RF-601 Package contents** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-10 - ADR-005
As a modernisation team I want a complete, standard package so that we can plan the work without asking.

- Given a produced package, when it is opened, then it contains a README with reading order, a requirements specification, a target architecture, ADRs for the main decisions, a mapping of legacy entities to target entities, a roadmap with phases and exit criteria, task cards with acceptance criteria, and a risks and open-questions list.
- Given the planner's answer, when it is read, then each item of its lists (requirements, components, decisions, entities, phases, risks, task cards and every `technical` list) is read on its own: an item that does not read (a field missing, of the wrong shape or out of its bounds) is left out of the package and listed in `checks.md` under "Left out" with its list, position and the field that failed, never with a word of the answer (P3-126).
- Given a planner answer in which no requirement reads, or whose lists are not lists or are longer than their bounds, when it is read, then it is refused; the planner gets one repair call that names the failing paths (list, position and field) before the run fails with `RST-1803`.
- Given the scan of the analysed commit (EV-32, P8-06), when the task cards are linked, then each module of the scan (a folder that holds a project or package manifest, outside the deployment material, with its manifests, languages and file count) is an item `module:<folder>` and the deployment material is the one item `deployment:source`; at most 60 modules, the largest first, are sent to the planner. A test project (a folder or project named `test`, `tests`, `spec`, `UnitTests` or `IntegrationTests`, or ending in `.Tests`, `.UnitTests` or a CamelCase `Tests`, `Test` or `Specs` such as `OrderServiceTests`; `Contest.Web`, `Attestation` and `TestHarness.App` stay modules) and vendored third-party code (`vendor`, `third_party`, `node_modules`, a `lib` folder under `wwwroot`, `static`, `public` or `Scripts`, or a file the scan marks vendored) are no modules. Deployment material is matched by exact names, never by a substring: a folder whose name has the whole word `kubernetes`, `k8s`, `helm` or `servicefabric`, or is `deploy`, `deployment` or `deployments` outside a module (only its configuration and script files count; such a folder holding a package manifest stays deployment when it holds no code, and is a module named in `checks.md` when it does); a Helm chart, found by its `Chart.yaml` whatever its folder is called, with everything under it; a file named `Dockerfile`, `Dockerfile.<variant>`, `docker-compose[.<variant>].yml`, `compose.yml`, a publish profile or a Docker or Service Fabric project. A folder that holds a manifest is a module, never deployment (`Foo.ServiceFabric`, `KubernetesClient`, `HelmetService`), and nothing under `docs` or `tests` at the root counts. A CI file (`.github/workflows/*.yml`, `azure-pipelines.yml`, `Jenkinsfile` and the like) may only build and test, so it is not counted as deployment. A task card touches a module, or the deployment material, only when the planner links it by its id (`coverage.v2`): a card that writes the new repository's own CI file or Dockerfile does not cover the legacy deployment. The gap call (`gap.v4`) and its repair (`gap-repair.v4`) ask for the modules and the deployment no card touches, by id: a card that ports or retires each module and one deployment card. A module or the deployment material still touched by no card is listed in `checks.md` under "Modules with no task card" with its folder, languages, file count and the reason (the plan has no phase, a card that named it was left out with its position and field, the call was refused, the planner wrote no card, or the module was over the cap); it is logged and never fails the run (RST-1806 stays for the items of RF-627). The test projects, vendored folders, `deploy` folders counted as modules, CI files and files that look like deployment but count nowhere (Terraform, Bicep, Docker-named or YAML files in an `infra`, `docker`, `charts` or `deploy` folder) are listed under "Folders and files not counted as modules" with the reason, at most 20 of a kind and then an "and N more" row, so none is dropped in silence. With every module touched, `checks.md` says so; with no readable scan there is no module item.
  Verification: schema and structure test of the package; manual review against the demo app.

**RF-602 Trace the plan to the code** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-19 - ADR-005
As a modernisation team I want every requirement in the package linked to its source cards and citations so that we can check any statement in the legacy code.

- Given a requirement in the package, when it is read, then it lists the card ids it comes from and those cards list their citations.
- Given a requirement, a feature, a business rule or a flow step that names an operation, when the package is written, then `checks.md` lists it under "Meaning of the source claims" when its verb contradicts the verb of a linked Supported claim on the same topic (delete against reject or archive, record against ignore, block against print; the pairs are the versioned catalogue file `claim-verbs.v<n>.yaml`, never code) and when it holds a number or a quoted value that no claim of its source cards states (P5-43).
- Given a planner prompt, when it asks for operations, roles or flow steps, then it tells the planner to keep the verb and values of the claim and to name only roles a claim states; the wording is in the versioned prompts `planner/package.v12.md`, `planner/prd.v4.md` and `planner/flows.v2.md` (RNF-004) (P5-43).
- Given a PRD role that no Supported claim states, when the package is written, then `prd.md` marks it as proposed and `checks.md` lists it with each statement that names it (P5-43).
- Given a claim that cites only documentation files and a claim that cites other files and says the opposite about the same topic, when the package is written, then the code claim is used, the documentation claim is not sent to the planner or written to the package, and `checks.md` lists both (P5-43).
  Verification: structure test: no requirement without a source card; unit and integration tests of the meaning check with recorded plans (P5-43).

**RF-603 Tool-agnostic output** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-10 - ADR-005
As a modernisation team I want plain Markdown with no dependency on Rosetta, a build tool or an AI vendor so that we can use it any way we like.

- Given the package, when it is read outside Rosetta, then it is plain Markdown and Mermaid, with no Rosetta-specific syntax.
  Verification: structure test.

**RF-604 Produce the hand-off package from the planning brief** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-72, DEC-80, DEC-82, DEC-142, DEC-166 - ADR-005, ADR-018
As a developer I want to start `plan` from the web UI once the planning brief is saved so that a team receives a package built the way we chose.

- Given the source understand run chosen in RF-614, with verified cards and answers, and a saved planning brief, when I start `plan`, then a `handoff/` folder is written using only `Supported` claims and answered questions; `Rejected`, invalid and unverified claims are listed as excluded with their ids in `claims-left-out.md` (P5-26).
- Given a source run with hundreds of claims left out (`Rejected`, `CitationInvalid`, `Unverified` or `Proposed`), when the package is written, then `open-questions.md` lists the questions that need an answer first (the brief, the product requirements document and the analysis), then a short summary of the claims left out with their counts per card kind, status and area and the 20 most important (business rules first, then features, then the other kinds, the most cited first), and links `claims-left-out.md`, which lists every one of them with its id, card, kind, area, citations and status and not their text; the file reads under 150 lines for 761 claims left out, and the files write a hyphen, a parenthesis, a dot or a `#` inside a word as it is while text from the code or the model stays inert (RF-507, P5-26).
- Given the source run ended `Completed` or `CapReached`, when I start `plan`, then it runs; given it ended `Failed` or `Interrupted`, or ended in any state with no `Supported` claim, then `plan` refuses it with `RST-1802` before any model call. Only these two end states can be planned from (DEC-166, which amends DEC-143 Q-B).
- Given the source run ended `CapReached`, when the package is written, then the first lines of its README and a section of `checks.md` say that the run stopped at a cap, and checks.md says how many of its claims were never judged (`Unverified` or `Proposed`) and are excluded; a package from a `Completed` run says nothing about a cap (DEC-166).
- Given a planning brief with unanswered questions, when `plan` runs, then each takes its default and the package lists it in its open-questions file with that default (DEC-80).
- Given a planning brief in which no stack layer is answered and the plan could propose none (the scan cannot be read, or the kind is keep as is), when `plan` writes the package, then the first line of its README says that no technology was chosen, that the cards are written for any stack, and that a stack is chosen in Plan setup; given one stack layer answered, that line is not written (Q-59, DEC-80). When the plan proposed layers (RF-609), the README says so instead.
- Given a planner answer whose `sources` entries are claim ids (`ENT-002.c4`) instead of card ids (EV-83, P8-02), when it is checked against the supported cards, then an entry counts as its card id (`ENT-002`) when that card is supported, in the requirements, the entity mappings and every other `sources` list; an entry that names no supported card, as a card or as a claim, is still dropped and counted in the warning. The package prompt (`package.v12`) says that `sources` holds card ids, not claim ids, with one example.
- Given a checked answer that keeps no requirement, or (given SDD) keeps no task card with a requirement because every requirement the cards name was left out (P8-02), when the check ends, then the planner gets one automatic repair round, metered and counted in the run like any planner call: the same message, the repair prompt (`plan-repair.v2`) and one line per item left out with its list, position, field and the reason, written by Rosetta and holding no text of the answer; the better of the two answers is used, and a run that still has no requirement fails with `RST-1803`, with no third call. A card left without a requirement while other cards keep theirs gets no round.
- Given SDD and a package task card whose `requirements` is empty or names only requirements that were left out (P8-02, RF-607), when the answer is checked, then the card is kept until the cards are linked to the items they build, and then takes every requirement that cites a source card behind what it builds (as a gap card does, P7-14); a card whose items no requirement cites is kept with each item noted in `checks.md`, and only a card that builds nothing with a source card is left out, logged and named in `checks.md` by its position, the other cards numbered again.
- Given a finished package, when its run manifest is read, then it records the planning brief, the catalogue version and the prompt versions used (RNF-004).
  Verification: integration test with recorded responses.

**RF-605 Plan setup page** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-72, DEC-80, DEC-142, DEC-178, DEC-179 - ADR-012, ADR-018
As a developer I want Rosetta to interview me before it plans, the way a senior architect would, so that the package follows my choices instead of guesses.

- Given a finished `understand` run, when I open Plan setup from it or from the project, then the page names the source run (RF-614) and the questions appear in small groups (principles, architecture, migration, stack, project context, build method), each with a default, the reason it matters and the option Rosetta recommends marked "(Recommended)".
- Given any Plan setup question (DEC-178, RF-645), when it is shown, then it offers at least three options, the recommended one first and marked "(Recommended)", each with a one-line description of what it means and its consequence, plus "Type your own answer"; the recommended option is the default that applies when the question is skipped, and a typed answer is saved in the brief.
- Given the questions of the source run and of its Deepens (RF-646, DEC-179), when Plan setup opens, then it uses the one question list and lists only the questions still open, with the answered ones in a collapsed "Answered" list.
- Given no stack layer is answered, when I look at "Use the defaults for the rest", then the page says beside it that the package will name no technology and the suggested stack options stay unused, with a link to the Stack step; once one layer is answered the line is not shown (Q-59, DEC-80).
- Given I accept the defaults or change some answers, when I save, then the planning brief is stored with the project (data-model.md section 3.20) and shown as a "What I recorded" summary that I can edit and save again.
