- Given the build method, when it is written, then it names a small set of measures (the share of cards passing review the first time, review rounds per card, cost or tokens per card, escaped defects found after merge), says where they are recorded, and says to change one thing in the process at a time and compare.
  Verification: structure test of the build method.

**RF-639 Agent tool and models per difficulty band** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-173, DEC-176 - ADR-018
As a team that builds with AI agents I want to say which agent tool and which models we have so that every task card names a model we can use.

- Given "Agents will build", when Plan setup asks, then it asks the agent tool (Claude Code, Codex or another, named in free text) and one model name per tier (smallest, middle, largest) and optionally an escalation model that is used only with the owner's approval in chat, each with its default and its example; for Claude Code the defaults are `haiku`, `sonnet` and `opus`, for Codex the defaults are `gpt-5-mini`, `gpt-5-codex` and `gpt-5`, and for another tool there are no default model names, so the question is required and the examples are placeholders such as "the cheapest, the middle and the strongest model of your plan".
- Given the answers, when the package is written, then every task card shows the model of its tier next to its difficulty, in the form `Difficulty: 6 (middle tier, model: sonnet)`, and names the verifier tier of its band (middle for difficulty 1-8, largest for 9-10); the bands stay 1-5 smallest, 6-8 middle, 9-10 largest.
- Given a tier with no model name, when the package is written, then the card shows the tier alone and the package check (RF-641) lists it in `checks.md`.
  Verification: unit test of the brief schema, integration test of the card line, e2e test of the step.
  Changed 2026-10-28 by DEC-185: the brief has an optional "runs locally" flag per tier model (additive), so the local-model warning of RF-641 can fire; card P6-22.
  Changed 2026-10-30 by DEC-200: Codex has the default tier models `gpt-5-mini`, `gpt-5-codex` and `gpt-5` (card P7-12); the default model names of every agent tool move to one versioned catalogue file under `prompts/catalogue` that the owner edits without a code change (card P7-23).

**RF-640 Start-here index and build protocol** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-173, DEC-176 - ADR-018
As an agent that opens the package I want to know where to start and how the team works so that no person has to explain it.

- Given "Agents will build", when the package is written, then `AGENTS.md` has at most about 60 lines (start-here order, commands, the rules broken most often, layout) and is first in the README reading order, and `orchestration.md` is second and contains: the roles (orchestrator, builder per tier, independent verifier per band), the band table with the models, the retry ladder (a second failure goes one band up, never down), the card status flow, the merge rules including `needs-owner`, the human gates, the usage throttle, escalation and stop conditions, the report and verdict formats, the red test first, and the size budgets of living files.
- Given "People will build", when the package is written, then `orchestration.md` is absent.
  Verification: structure test of both files; the line budget of `AGENTS.md` is a test.
  Changed 2026-10-28 by DEC-186: the long rules (tiers, quality, behaviour, operations, metrics) stay in `AGENTS.md`; the "about 60 lines" budget applies to its start-here index part only, RF-636 keeps operations in `AGENTS.md`, and `orchestration.md` holds the build protocol.
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-190: the per-language grep patterns of the hard-rule table in `orchestration.md` and the aliases that map a stack answer to a language (including `dotnet` and `jvm`) move to a versioned catalogue file under `prompts/catalogue` (RNF-004); a rule that a chosen principle states but that has no pattern for the stack is listed as "checked by reading" (card P6-28).

**RF-641 Package check of the model per card** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-173, DEC-176, RF-635 - ADR-018
As a lead I want the package to catch a card given a model the team does not have, or too weak a tier, so that nobody finds out in the middle of the build.

- Given "Agents will build", when `checks.md` is written, then it lists, with the card id and without correcting anything, a card whose model is not one of the chosen tool's models (or its escalation model with the owner-approval note), whose tier is lower than its band, whose risk class other than `none` has a tier lower than the middle one or a verifier lower than the strongest, whose verifier is of a lower tier than its builder, or whose risk class is not shown next to its difficulty.
- Given a local model listed as a builder for a card of difficulty above 2, when `checks.md` is written, then the check lists a warning for that card.
- Given a card whose verifier tier runs locally and whose difficulty is 3 or above, when the package is written, then the card's Verifier line says "runs locally", the band table and `agents/README.md` say so too, and `checks.md` lists one warning for the package that a local verifier is weaker than a hosted one and suggests a hosted verifier for cards of difficulty 3 and above.
- Given a failed check, when the package is written, then it is still written: the check lists a finding and never blocks, like the other checks of `checks.md`.
  Verification: unit tests of each failure and of the warning; a clean package lists none.
  Changed 2026-10-28 by DEC-185: the brief's "runs locally" flag (card P6-22) feeds the local-model warning, and a card that uses the escalation model shows "escalation, owner approved" on its card line (card P6-23).
  Changed 2026-11-01 by DEC-201 (card P7-24): a local verifier is named as running locally on the card, in the band table and in `agents/README.md`, and the check gives one package-level warning for a local verifier of a card of difficulty 3 and above; the package is still written.
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-192, replacing the escalation part of the note above (DEC-185 item 7): no card gets the escalation model when the plan is made and no card line shows escalation; only at a card's third verifier FAIL, with the owner's approval in chat, does the orchestrator write "escalation, owner approved" on that card's Status line and use the escalation model. The retry ladder of `orchestration.md` (RF-640) states that exact Status-line text, and the check accepts the escalation model only on a card whose Status line carries it and otherwise lists the card (card P6-23).
  Changed 2026-10-30 by DEC-200: a tier whose model runs locally is given difficulties 1 and 2 only; the cards above go to the next tier, and the band table and `agents/README.md` say the tier runs locally (card P7-12).
  Changed 2026-10-30 by DEC-201: a local model may be the verifier of a band; then the card's Verifier line, the band table and `agents/README.md` say it runs locally, and `checks.md` lists a warning that a local verifier is weaker than a hosted one and suggests a hosted verifier for difficulty 3 and above (card P7-24).

**RF-642 Agent definitions per tool** - R1 - Could - Source DEC-173, DEC-176, DEC-60 - ADR-018
As a team that builds with Claude Code or Codex I want the role definitions ready to copy so that we start with the same roles this method uses.

- Given "Agents will build" and a tool of Claude Code or Codex, when the package is written, then an `agents/` folder holds one definition per role for that tool (orchestrator, a builder per tier, a verifier per band), each with the model of its tier, and a note on where to copy it; and the same roles are described in prose in `orchestration.md`, so the package is usable without the tool. Nothing in the package installs, runs or configures the tool.
- Given another tool, when the package is written, then there is no `agents/` folder and the roles are described in prose in `orchestration.md` only.
- Given the brief names an escalation model, when the package is written for Claude Code, then an escalation definition is included with the note that it is used only with the owner's approval; without a named escalation model there is no such file.
  Verification: structure test per tool; a test that the folder contains no hook, command or plugin file (DEC-60).
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-190: the package README reading order and `AGENTS.md` link `agents/README.md`; `orchestration.md` says that another tool gets the roles in prose only; the package has no escalation verifier definition (card P6-30).

**RF-643 Recommended delivery flow** - R1 - Could - Source DEC-173, DEC-175, DEC-176 - ADR-018
As a team that will host the new application I want a suggested branch and release flow so that a change reaches users only after it was tried.

- Given "Agents will build" and a project context field "deployment" that says the new application is hosted, when `orchestration.md` is written, then it has a "Recommended delivery flow" section (integration branch, staging with a smoke test, owner-merged release) marked as a recommendation the team may replace by a decision it records.
- Given the field is absent or says the application is not hosted, then the section is absent.
  Verification: structure test of both cases.

**RF-644 Lessons from running a build** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-177 - ADR-018
As a team building with AI agents I want the package to carry the lessons earlier builds paid for so that we do not learn them again.

- Given "Agents will build", when `orchestration.md` is written, then it has a "Lessons from running a build" section, tool-neutral, with each of these items in one or two sentences: continuous integration on the pull request is the authority and a result is read from the exit code, never from a search of output; a flaky test may hide a real defect and becomes a card (flaky focus or keyboard end-to-end steps usually mean the page heading renders only after data loads, so the Done-when of a user-interface card includes "the heading renders before data loads"); verifier minors that are pure wording may be fixed by the orchestrator on the branch before merge and everything else becomes a follow-up card; the owner's hands-on review changes the product, so each change is recorded as a decision, withdrawn requirements are marked, requirement ids are added and then cards are cut; an owner's bug report is traced in the logs, reproduced by a failing test and fixed in a follow-up card (a cause in seeded data fixes the seed and recreates the local database after asking); patterns the secrets scan looks for are written self-safe in documents (for example `Passwor[d]=`) and not allow-listed; parallel cards collide in shared files (resource files, allow-lists, shared test files), which are resolved by union with a clean full build after each merge; cards with date navigation test the first and last supported dates; verifiers work only in the branch's worktree and the main checkout is checked after each; a feature branch is built for the owner to try when the owner asks to try a change before it is merged; agents never skip or override hooks, add files by explicit path, never use a bare stash, stop processes only by the id they started, and install without lifecycle scripts in shared worktrees; one writer per seat, with full paths and an own log folder per agent and a lease file when a second orchestrator could take over; the product is evaluated on real inputs in a separate stack with its own ports and data, with an id per finding; near a deadline the scope is frozen to what stops the journey, gives wrong content or loses data; the work is shown at the end of a phase; the session end updates the living hand-off and checks it for stale claims; and clean-up after every merge removes the card's worktrees and branches, keeping a worktree a running container stack uses.
- Given the brief names Claude Code as the agent tool, when the section is written, then it adds that agent definitions load only from the session's project folder, so otherwise a general-purpose subagent is told to read the builder or verifier file; for another tool this item is absent.
- Given the brief's platform is Windows, when the section is written, then it adds Windows hygiene (reserved TCP port ranges, the command-line length limit of `npx`, a running app locks build output, the container engine stops with its Linux subsystem, overnight system restarts); given the brief's stack is .NET, then it adds that a build locked by a running preview is built in another configuration or after stopping the preview; for any other platform or stack these items are absent.
- Given the section, then it names no employer and no other project (DEC-03). The Workflow-script rules of the project-blueprint skill are left out because they concern the skill's own tooling, not a build team's, and "disjoint file groups" is already covered by RF-634.
  Verification: structure test of the generated `orchestration.md` (the always-items present; the tool- and platform-specific items present only when the brief matches; no employer or project names).
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-190: until the brief has a platform field, the Windows items key on the stack answers: an answer that names Windows or IIS counts as Windows, and "Windows Authentication" alone does not; each Windows item states only the fact named above, and the locked-build-output item uses the wording of the .NET item (card P6-29).

**RF-645 Suggested answers for every question** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-178 - ADR-005, ADR-012, ADR-018
As a user I want every question Rosetta asks to offer suggested answers, the way Claude Code asks, so that I can answer with one choice and see what each choice means.

- Given an `OQ` card, when it is written, then it carries at least three suggested answers, one marked recommended and listed first, each with a short description of what it means and its consequence and, when the code supports it, a citation; a card without them is refused with a repair message like the one of RF-243, a card saved before DEC-178 is exempt, and the recommended answer is the default that applies when the question is skipped. A question with only two natural answers adds a third qualified answer (for example "yes, with a condition"), never filler.
- Given the Your questions tab and the answers page, when an `OQ` card with suggested answers is shown, then the suggested answers are choices with the recommended one first, plus "Type your own answer"; choosing one writes it to `answers.md` with which option was chosen (data-model.md section 3.16); open questions saved before DEC-178 keep their free answer field and show no suggested answers.
- Given any Plan setup question, when it is shown, then it offers at least three options, the recommended one first, plus "Type your own answer", and a typed answer is saved in the brief (data-model.md section 3.20).
- Given the Stack step, when it is shown, then each layer is a group of option cards (`PlanChoice`, the recommended one first marked "(Recommended)") with "Type your own answer" (`PlanOwnChoice`) last, as in the other steps, and a layer that offers fewer than two proposals says "up to two" in the step text, not "two proposals" (DEC-208, DEC-211, P8-21; `tests/unit/web/plan-setup-stack-platform.test.tsx`).
- Given a saved brief, when Plan setup is reloaded, then every saved Stack layer is shown: a layer whose choice is one of the offered options selects that option's radio, and a layer whose choice is not offered (whatever its `custom` flag) selects "Type your own answer" with its text filled, so saving without a change stores the same brief; and when the brief is saved, a stack choice that is not among the `offered` options the page listed for it is stored with `custom` true (card P8-22, UX-02).
- Given the package's open questions (RF-637), when they are written, then each lists at least three suggested answers with the recommended one marked and says a team may answer otherwise.
- Given the wording of the fixed Plan setup options (the project-context answers, the build method and keeping the legacy technology), when the page and the package show them, then both use one source, which the server serves with the catalogue (P7-17).
- Given a brief saved since card P6-21 with a typed answer to principles, architecture, migration or build method (DEC-187), when the plan runs, then the text reaches the planner only inside the owner's labelled data block, as user context and never as evidence or an instruction (ADR-013), and the question leaves the defaulted list; the architecture or migration decision record is Accepted and states the owner's answer; the package leads with the own answer and names the enumerated default only as the reference that Rosetta's dependency check (style) or strategy-fit check (strategy) uses, not the chosen one; a typed principle is listed beside the ticked principles, which are still sent; a typed build method is stated in the Build method section of `open-questions.md`.
- Given a typed migration answer, when the package is written, then the cut-over card keeps its id, place and dependencies but prescribes no strategy: it points to the migration decision record, every part moves the way that answer describes, every move can be undone and the legacy system is switched off only after the last part is live; the strategy-fit check of RF-610 reports against the default strategy as the reference and `checks.md` says so; and no shared-database seam record is written.
- Given a brief saved before card P6-21 that holds a typed answer, when it is read, then the question stays in the defaulted list until the brief is saved again, and "What I recorded" reads "Recorded; the plan uses the default until you save the brief again"; after a save it reads "Recorded; the planner reads it as your own answer".
- Given an `OQ` card whose question is not a yes/no question (it starts with who, whom, whose, which, where, what, when, why or how), when a suggested answer starts with "Yes" or "No", then the card is refused with a repair message and the agent gives the third answer the same kind as the question; only a yes/no question gets a qualified "Yes, but ..." (card P7-21, EV-69).
- Given an `OQ` card whose question starts with "In which" or "For whom" (a preposition and a question word), or a Who question whose suggested answer starts with "No one", when it is parsed, then it is an open question that takes "No one ..." as an answer, and a line break, a tab or an invisible character between two words of a suggested answer or its description is one space, never nothing (EV-89, P8-08).
- Given an `OQ` card whose question clearly offers a choice between two sides (a question that starts with a verb such as is, are, do, does or can and repeats the same preposition on both sides: "Is it run by this application's team or by another system?"), when a suggested answer starts with "Yes" or "No", then the card is kept and only that answer is dropped, provided the recommended answer stays and at least three answers remain; otherwise the card is kept as written. "or" between two verbs or single words ("cancel or return", "Spain or Portugal"), "or both", "or not" and "and/or" are not sides, and a plain yes/no question keeps its qualified "Yes, but ...". The reader format `card-format.v6` tells the model to name a side in each answer (card P9-13, EV-89, EV-57, EV-69).
- Given a typed architecture answer in Plan setup, when it is saved, then the brief keeps `recommended` and the evidence of the styles the planner proposed; the ADR of the own answer names Rosetta's recommendation and lists the proposed styles as options considered, and the style check of `checks.md` judges the components against the owner's own answer and does not fall back to Layered (card P7-21, EV-73).
- Given a stack layer the owner left open and Rosetta proposed (the flag of a proposed layer), when the grep table is written, then a language named only by that layer adds no patterns to it, and the table says which proposed layer's patterns were left out (card P7-21).
  Verification: unit test of the card schema and parser, end-to-end test of both pages, structure test of the package.
  Changed 2026-10-28 by DEC-184: the build method question has no recommended option, an explicit exception to the recommended-first rule (DEC-176, RF-612); a typed answer to principles, architecture, migration or build method is stored in the brief and shown in "What I recorded", and until card P6-21 passes it to the planner inside a labelled data block (ADR-013) the question stays in the defaulted list (the plan uses the default, the ADR stays Proposed, the open question is kept). Superseded 2026-10-29 by DEC-187: card P6-21 is done, so the typed answer reaches the planner, the question leaves the defaulted list and the own-answer record is Accepted, not Proposed (the scenarios above).
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-187: a typed build method always gives a people-build package; the API refuses a brief that carries a typed build method together with `aiAssisted: true` (card P6-32). Typed stack and project-context answers keep being used by the plan. By DEC-189, the package's open questions list "at least three" suggested answers (RF-637, card P6-31).
  Changed 2026-10-30 by DEC-200: a blank tier-model question of Plan setup lists the suggested answers from the model map of the brief's tool (card P7-12).

**RF-648 How people sign in to a rebuilt workbook** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-150, DEC-178 - ADR-005, ADR-018
As the owner of a workbook I want Plan setup to ask how people will sign in so that the package names a sign-in method instead of leaving it as "Approach: other", because a workbook has no sign-in to read from code.

- Given a project whose source is Excel workbooks (DEC-150), when Plan setup opens the project-context step, then it asks "How will people sign in to the new application?" with three options and "Type your own answer", each with a one-line description (RF-645): Windows authentication, single sign-on with the organisation's identity provider, and accounts of the application with passwords; the recommended option is first and marked "(Recommended)": Windows authentication when the answer to the hosting layer names IIS, Windows or an intranet, single sign-on otherwise.
- Given the question is skipped, when the brief is read, then the recommended option applies, the package marks it as a default and `open-questions.md` lists the question with the three options (RF-637).
- Given a workbook plan with an answer, when the plan runs, then the answer reaches the planner only inside the owner's labelled data block (ADR-013); `trd.md` names it in the security section instead of "Approach: other" for a control about signing in, and a decision record "Sign-in" states it; a project whose source is a repository or a zip is not asked, and its package is unchanged.
- Given a control of the technical requirements whose approach is "other", when the project is a workbook, then it names the sign-in or the secrets answer only if the control's own title is about signing in or about secrets; a "CSRF token" or "password hashing" control, or one that only mentions signing in in its text, keeps "Approach: other" (P8-09).
- Given a project that is not a workbook (a repository or a zip), when a save carries a `workbook.*` key in `chosen` or `typed`, then the save is refused with a problem for that key; the labels and options of the three questions come from the wording the server serves with the catalogue (P8-09).
  Verification: unit tests of the options and the default, of the brief and the owner's block, and of `trd.md`; web test of the question; integration test of a recorded workbook plan.

**RF-649 Where the secrets of a rebuilt workbook go** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-150, DEC-178 - ADR-005, ADR-018
As the owner of a workbook I want Plan setup to ask where the secrets found in the workbook go so that the package names a store and the cards stop holding them in code or settings files.

- Given a project whose source is Excel workbooks, when Plan setup opens the project-context step, then it asks "Where will the new application keep its secrets?" with three options and "Type your own answer", each with a one-line description (RF-645): the secret store of the hosting platform (recommended, first), a dedicated secrets manager, and environment variables set by the deployment.
- Given the question is skipped, when the brief is read, then the hosting platform's secret store applies, the package marks it as a default and `open-questions.md` lists the question with its options.
- Given a workbook plan with an answer, when the plan runs, then the answer reaches the planner only inside the owner's labelled data block (ADR-013); `trd.md` names the store in the security section instead of "Approach: other" for a control about secrets, and a decision record "Secrets store" states it; a repository or zip project is not asked and its package is unchanged.
- Given a secret typed as the answer to "Where will the new application keep its secrets?", when the brief is saved and the plan runs, then the secret is masked in `planning-brief.json`, in the message to the planner and in every file of the package (P8-09, RNF-003).
  Verification: as RF-648.

**RF-650 Screens for each register of a rebuilt workbook** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-150, DEC-178 - ADR-005, ADR-018
As the owner of a workbook I want Plan setup to ask whether each register gets screens so that `flows/` and `ux/` of the package are not empty for a workbook, which has no screens to read.

- Given a project whose source is Excel workbooks, when Plan setup opens the project-context step, then it asks "Should each register get screens?" with three options and "Type your own answer", each with a one-line description (RF-645): list, edit and search screens for each register (recommended, first), list and search screens only, and no screens.
- Given the question is skipped, when the brief is read, then list, edit and search screens apply, the package marks them as a default and `open-questions.md` lists the question with its options.
- Given a workbook plan whose answer is not "no screens", when the package is written, then `ux/` lists one screen set per table of the data model with its fields (list and search, and edit when the answer allows it) and `flows/` lists a sign-in journey and one journey per table; with "no screens" both say that no screen was asked for; a repository or zip project is not asked and its package is unchanged.
  Verification: as RF-648.

**RF-646 One question list, asked once before planning** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-179 - ADR-005, ADR-012, ADR-018
As a user I want each question asked once, after the last Deepen, so that my answers are used and never requested again.

- Given an understand run and the Deepens linked to it, when their questions are listed, then they form one list for the project, consolidated by the merge record of the cards (RF-1007) and by asking-the-same (Q-52); a new open question that asks the same as an answered one is merged into it and keeps the answer, so an answered question is never asked again, and an unanswered one raised again is merged, not duplicated.
- Given an understand run that can be deepened, when the journey (RF-615) or the "Your turn" panel (RF-1017) shows the next actions, then they recommend Deepen first and say that answers given now will be used by it; the user is asked to answer the questions after the last Deepen, or right away when the user chooses not to Deepen; answering earlier stays possible from the Your questions tab.
- Given Plan setup (RF-605) or the plan inputs (RF-614), when they list questions, then they show only the questions still open, with the answered ones in a collapsed "Answered" list that can be edited, and the plan uses the one list.
- Given questions saved before DEC-179, when they are read, then duplicates across a run and its Deepens are merged on read by the same rule, keeping every answer; when two answers conflict the newer one wins and the older is kept in the history of `answers.md`, and no section is deleted (RF-1007).
  Verification: integration test with recorded responses for a Deepen that uses answers and does not ask again; end-to-end test of the order of the journey.
  Changed 2026-10-29 by DEC-189: the questions are asked once at least one Deepen of the run has ended, as built in P6-19; an answer saved but not yet used by a run was kept with the open questions, labelled "Answer saved" (RF-615; replaced by DEC-211, see below); the "Do not Deepen" choice stays in the browser's local storage as a per-browser convenience.
  Changed 2026-11-09 by DEC-211 (P8-85): a saved answer moves its question to the collapsed "Answered" list at once, still labelled "Answer saved" until a run uses it; it no longer stays with the open questions (RF-1007).
  Changed 2026-11-09 by DEC-211 (P8-84): Deepen first only while own code was read below the coverage bar; at the bar or above, "Your turn" offers Set up the plan as its one primary button and Deepen as a link, and the journey of the run page asks the questions without recommending Deepen (RF-1021). The project page, which does not read the coverage, keeps this rule.

**RF-647 Questions and plan setup on one screen** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-178, DEC-204 - ADR-005, ADR-018, ADR-027
As a user I want the open questions and the plan setup on one screen with the recommended answers filled in so that I accept them with one click and change only what I care about.

- Given a run with open questions (RF-646) and the plan setup questions (RF-614), when I open the screen, then it lists both, each filled in with its recommended answer (RF-645) and marked as recommended; the main button is "Accept the recommended answers".
- Given a changed answer, when I save, then my answer is kept, `answers.md` and the planning brief are written as today (RF-615), and the screen shows the plan's estimate with a "Start the plan" button.
- Given the saved brief, when the screen is shown, then the plan does not start until I press "Start the plan" (DEC-204); the estimate card names that button "Start the plan" (it was "Write the package") and "Start the plan again" when the package is out of date.
- Given an open question with suggested answers that has no saved answer, when the screen is shown, then its recommended option is chosen and marked "(Recommended)"; when I press "Accept the recommended answers", then each such question is saved on the option it shows (a changed option is saved as changed, a saved answer is not written again), the planning brief is written with the defaults for what was not answered, and a question that fails to save stops the click before the brief is written (P7-36).
  Verification: web tests; an end-to-end test where a run with three open questions reaches a saved brief in one screen and one click.

### 5.8 Milestone hand-in artefacts (RF-800..RF-899)

**RF-800 Publish the sample report** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-04 - ADR-002
As the product owner I want the demo run's report published on GitHub Pages so that the milestone has a deployment URL.

- Given a finished demo run, when the publish step runs, then the report is copied to `site/` as static HTML in which repository and model text is escaped (RF-507), with a `.nojekyll` file so GitHub Pages renders nothing itself, served for the public repository, and the README links it (DEC-112).
  Verification: manual check of the public URL.

**RF-801 Reproducible quick start** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-04, DEC-61 - ADR-014
As a reviewer I want the README to reproduce the demo locally so that I can check the tool works without the hosted server.

- Given a clean machine with Docker and Ollama, when I follow the README quick start (`.env` from `.env.example`, `docker compose up`, sign in with the bootstrap admin), then the demo `scan` and `understand` on the chosen area complete.
  Verification: manual walk-through on a clean folder.

**RF-802 Slides and video** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-04 - -
As the product owner I want slides and a video that present Rosetta so that the milestone hand-in is complete.

- Given the milestone, when it is handed in, then the slides URL and the video URL are linked from the README; the format follows Q-02 and Q-03.
  Verification: manual check of both URLs.

**RF-803 Hosted URL and teacher account for the hand-in** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-67 - ADR-014, ADR-015
As the product owner I want the hand-in to give the evaluators a working URL and an account so that they can use Rosetta themselves.

- Given the hosted deployment (RF-1304), when the milestone is handed in, then the deployment URL field holds the hosted URL, the test user field holds the teacher account's user name and password (RF-1107), and the GitHub Pages sample report (RF-800) is listed as a fallback.
  Verification: manual sign-in with the teacher account on the hosted URL the day before the hand-in.

### 5.9 Web UI (RF-1000..RF-1099)

**RF-1000 Start the web UI** - Withdrawn 2026-10-11 - see RF-1300 and RF-1301 (DEC-59, DEC-61: the server starts with the container)

**RF-1001 Start a run from the browser** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-47, DEC-48 - ADR-012
As a developer I want to paste a GitHub URL and start a run from the page so that I never need the terminal for a demo.

- Given the start form, when I paste a URL, choose the stage and areas and press Start, then the page shows the estimate (RF-425) and the cloud warning when it applies (RF-143) and starts the run only after I confirm.
- Given an invalid or private URL, when I press Start, then the page shows the error code and message next to the field and starts nothing.
- Given a likely estimate above the cap that remains for this run, today or this month, when the start page shows it, then a warning names the cap and how far the estimate exceeds it; Start stays available and the budget guard stops the run cleanly at the cap (DEC-108).
  Verification: end-to-end test with a fake GitHub client and the fake provider.

**RF-1002 Live agent view** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-46 - ADR-012
As a developer I want to see every agent the run spawns and what it is doing so that the process is transparent.

- Given a running `understand`, when agents are spawned, then the page shows one panel per agent with its area, role, model, state, turn count, the tool call in progress (for example `read_file Catalog/Edit.aspx.cs:40-80`), files read, cards found so far, and tokens and cost, all updating live.
- Given the orchestrator, when the run progresses, then an overview shows agents planned, running, finished and failed, and a verbose event log lists every event with its time.
- Given an understand run page, when the stepper is shown, then it has the steps Snapshot, Scan, Understand and Verify; a Report step appears, done, only once a report exists for the run, and never shows as "waiting" (P8-11, EV-90).
- Given a running run, when the page shows its agents, then a line per role says how many calls that role has in flight now and the limit, for example "Verifier · 4 at once (limit 4)" (the reader and the verifier use `agents.parallelism`); it starts at 0, reads "idle" when nothing is in flight, and follows the run's events, also when the page is reloaded (P8-19). The count comes from `agent.started` and `agent.ended` for readers, and `run.ended` empties it, so a reader whose `agent.ended` was lost is not counted after a resume (P8-143); for the verifier it is the size of a set of claim ids: add the `claimId` at `verify.claim.started`, remove it at `verify.claim.callEnded` (an id that is not in the set changes nothing), and empty the set at `verify.judgement.started`, `verify.judgement.ended` and `run.ended`, so a log without these types counts 0 (`docs/data-model.md` 3.9); the limit comes from `run.started`.
- Given a reader that a Stop (or a shutdown) cut short, when its `agent.ended` is written and shown, then its state is `Stopped`, not `Failed`; a reader that a cap or the approval pause cut short (so also after "Stop here") ends `StoppedByCap`. The overview and the stepper count both as stopped ("1 of 2 agents done, 1 stopped"), never as failed, and a reader that really failed still counts as failed. A stopped reader that a resume read again under another task counts in neither figure, so a Completed resumed run shows no failed agent unless one failed; only a task of a later life of the run (after a `run.started`) is such a re-read, a later task of the same life (a Deepen round) is another read and the stop stays counted (P9-14). A stopped area with no card still gets its gap tile (P9-04, EV-104, DEC-217).
- Given an understand run that starts, when `run.started` is written, then its data carries `parallelism`, the number of reader tasks and of verifier judgements the run runs at once: `agents.parallelism` brought inside its range of 1 to 8 as the run uses it (DEC-117, RF-301), so a setting of 20 records 8 and a setting of 0 records 1. A `run.started` stored without `parallelism` (an event log written before it, or a run whose project cannot be read) still parses and replays, and the role line then shows the count and no limit (P8-59).
  Verification: end-to-end test driven by recorded events; manual check during a real run.

**RF-1003 Live cost and budget** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-13, DEC-46 - ADR-007, ADR-012
As a developer I want the cost meter in the page so that I can stop a run that costs too much.

- Given a running run, when calls complete, then the page shows tokens and cost per role and in total, the cap as a bar, and the estimate for comparison; a Stop button cancels the run cleanly (RF-422 semantics).
- Given a run that is a step of a journey with an approved amount (RF-434, DEC-205, DEC-206), when its page shows the header meter, the spend panel and the run meter, then each shows the one amount, what is left of the approved amount ("$3.96 left of $4.00 approved", in tokens for a journey on local models), counted live with the run's spend, and none shows the project run cap or the project token cap, which the approved amount replaces (RF-435); a run outside a journey keeps showing its run cap (P8-24, UX-21, UX-27, EV-85).
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events and the fake provider.

**RF-1004 Live verification view** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-46 - ADR-005, ADR-012
As a developer I want to watch claims move through their statuses so that I see how reliable the result is.

- Given verification running, when verdicts arrive, then each claim's status changes live with its reason and a link to the cited lines.
- Given the milestone, when only the citation check (RF-300) exists, then claims that passed it are labelled "awaiting model verification" until the model step (RF-301, P2) is built (DEC-103).
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events.

**RF-1005 Live coverage map** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-46 - ADR-005, ADR-012
As a developer I want a map of the repository coloured by what agents have read so that gaps are visible.

- Given a running run, when files are read, then a tree or grid of the analysed files fills in by area and by read share.
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events.

**RF-1006 Publish and store run events** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-46 - ADR-012
As a developer I want every run event streamed to the page and stored so that the page is live and the report can replay it.

- Given any run, when an event occurs, then it is published to every connected page through server-sent events and appended to `events.jsonl` in the run folder, in order, with a sequence number and time.
- Given a page that connects late or reconnects, when it subscribes, then it receives the events it missed from the stored log before live ones.
- Given a page that connects after the run's row ended and before its `run.ended` was stored, when it subscribes, then it receives that `run.ended` live; for a run that never logs it (its write failed) the stream ends after 5 seconds without it, and no `run.ended` is invented (P9-04, P9-14).
  Verification: integration tests of the event sink adapters.

**RF-1007 Answer open questions in the page** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-15, DEC-46, DEC-178, DEC-179 - ADR-012
As a developer I want to answer `OQ` cards in the page so that the loop is visual.

- Given open questions, when I answer one in the page, then `answers.md` is updated and the question is marked answered on the next re-run.
- Given a run whose consolidation merged open questions (P5-11), when the answers page, the plan inputs and package, the report or the journey list or count its questions, then they list the same set: the questions that survived consolidation, matched to the file's sections by the merge record of the cards and never by the words of a question. An answer filed under a merged-away question shows on its survivor unless the survivor has its own, a survivor with no section can be answered, a merged-away question is not listed and cannot be answered or reopened, and no section of `answers.md` is deleted.
- Given an `OQ` card with suggested answers (RF-645), when the answers page shows it, then the suggested answers are choices with the recommended one first, plus "Type your own answer"; choosing one writes it to `answers.md` with which option was chosen, and a question saved before DEC-178 keeps its free answer field.
- Given the questions of an understand run and of the Deepens linked to it (RF-646, DEC-179), when the page lists them, then it shows one list merged by the same merge record, the open questions first and the answered ones in a collapsed "Answered" list that can be edited.
- Given an understand run with open questions, when the Questions step of the journey says who asked them, then it reads "asked by the understand" ("3 open questions, asked by the understand") while no Deepen of the run has ended, "asked after the Deepen that is running" while one runs, and "asked after the last Deepen" only once a Deepen has ended (P8-85, UX-24).
- Given the questions of a run on the Your questions tab or in Plan setup, when they are listed, then each list is in id order (OQ-001, OQ-002, OQ-003), never newest first: the questions that wait for an answer first, and the ones with an answer in the collapsed "Answered" list (P8-85, UX-25).
- Given a question that waits for an answer, when its answer is saved, then it moves to the collapsed "Answered" list at once, without a reload, reading "Answer saved" with the message of the save until a run uses the answer, and the questions left to answer are counted again; a question whose answer is cleared moves back to the questions that wait (P8-85, UX-25).
- Given the questions of a run on the Your questions tab, when they are listed, then one bar under the questions that wait, above the collapsed "Answered" list, holds "Save answers (N)" and "Accept the recommended answers", as the P8Run artboard draws them; N counts the answers changed and not yet saved, in either list, and a question has no Save button of its own. A question that waits starts with no option chosen, its recommended option marked "(Recommended)" (P8-86, UX-24).
- Given several changed answers, when I press "Save answers (N)", then one request writes them all to `answers.md`; every question saved moves to its list at once and reads the message of its save, the status line reads "Saved. N questions wait for an answer.", and the focus goes to the first control of the next question that waits, or to the "Answered" summary when none waits. With nothing changed the button writes nothing and the status line reads "No answer has changed." (P8-86, UX-24).
- Given questions that wait with suggested answers, when I press "Accept the recommended answers", then each of them is saved on its recommended option in the same one request, as Plan setup does (RF-647): an answer I changed is saved as changed, a saved answer is not written again, and the other changed answers are saved with them. The button is shown only while a question with suggested answers waits; Plan setup keeps its own "Accept the recommended answers", which also writes the brief, and shows only "Save answers (N)" in its list (P8-86, UX-24).
- Given a save of several answers that the server refuses, when it fails, then no answer is written, the error is shown beside the two buttons, and the changed answers stay in their fields to be saved again (P8-86).
  Verification: end-to-end test.

**RF-1008 Browse runs and download** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-24, DEC-46 - ADR-012
As a developer I want the page to list past runs and open their reports and zip downloads so that everything is in one place.

- Given previous runs in the output folder, when I open the history, then each run shows its source, commit, stage, state, cost and links to its report and zip (RF-008).
- Given a run that I stopped with Stop, when it is listed in the history, on its run page or in the journey strip, then it reads "Stopped by you" and never "Failed" or "Interrupted" (P8-87).
- Given a run that ended `Interrupted`, when it is listed in the history or the project list, on its run page (live, and after a reload) or in the journey strip, then its end reason decides the wording: "Stopped by you" for a Stop on a running run, and for a run with no recorded reason; "Stopped by a restart" when the server restarted or shut down under it; "Cancelled while queued" when it was cancelled before it started. None of them reads "Failed" or "Interrupted", and a restart or a queue cancel never reads "Stopped by you" (P8-111, DEC-217).
- Given an ended run, when the history lists it, then a "Running time" column shows the sum of its active spans (`8 s`, `2 min`, `66 min`, and `2 h 10 min` from two hours on, as the artboard draws it and the project page reads it) and the start in the Run cell is its first start; a run that was resumed or continued and whose wall time, from its first start to its end, is at least one minute longer than its running time also shows the wall time after it as "(over 2 h 10 min)". A run that has not ended shows a dash. A run that ran 66 minutes in all, in two spans, with a stop in between that lasted 64 minutes, reads "66 min (over 2 h 10 min)", never the 13.8 minutes of its last span. The running time counts only the time the run was running: not the time it waited in the queue, paused for an answer or stopped, and not the time the server was down (a run that a restart ended counts its last span up to its last recorded event); the wall time, from the first start to the end, counts the waits after the first start (a wait in the queue before it is in neither time). A run that ended before the running time was recorded has one span, so its running time is its wall time and it shows no "(over ...)" (P8-88, P8-122, P8-123, EV-93).
- Given the history of a project, when it lists its runs, then the columns are Run, Kind, Commit (only for a GitHub project), State, Coverage, Started, Running time, Tokens, Cost and a row menu, as the History and cost artboard draws them; every value stays in its own column: the run name is the link to the run page, the start has its own column, and the measures line of an ended understand run is the Coverage column, never a line under another figure (P8-89, UX-19, EV-94).
- Given a Deepen run, when the history lists it, then its Kind reads "Deepen" with its rounds ("Deepen, 3 rounds", "Deepen, 1 round"; plain "Deepen" while it has not finished a round); an understand run that is not a Deepen reads "Understand", a scan "Scan" and a plan "Plan". The Cost page names the kinds with the same words (P8-89, P8-109, UX-06).
- Given an ended understand run that read an area below the coverage bar (RF-230), when the History table or the project page lists it, then its State is the state of its run page, the "Partly read" badge with "16% of your code" on the line under it, and not "Completed"; the table never grows wider for it (no sideways scroll of the page and every row's More actions inside the viewport at 1280 px and on a phone, in the light and dark views; and at 1024 px, where the table fits its box as the next Given says), and the figures come from the history answer, so the browser makes no request per row (P8-130).
- Given the History of a GitHub project with a scan and an understand run that is Partly read, when it is shown at 1024 px (a box about 760 px wide beside the open navigation), then the Runs table fits its box: it keeps every column the History and cost artboard draws (Run, Kind, Commit, State, Coverage, Started, Running time, Tokens, Cost and the row menu), its box does not scroll sideways, and every row's More actions ends inside the box and the viewport with the box not scrolled. From 641 px to 1279 px the cells sit closer together and a value wraps between its words or at its hyphens instead of widening the table: a column heading ("Running time"), the run name (its date, its time and its kind), a Deepen's rounds, the date and the time of the start, the wall time after the running time, the share under a Partly read badge, and a Resume button above the row menu; the commit, the coverage value and the figures never wrap. A state badge may wrap onto two lines and a Resume button sits above the row menu, so even the widest row (Well covered, "Stopped by a restart" and a Resume button) fits the box and every More actions is in view. At 1280 px and wider the table is as before, and on a phone it stays stacked (RNF-010) (P8-136).
- Given a project whose source is a zip or workbooks, when the history lists its runs, then it has no Commit column and no "none"; given a GitHub project, then each run shows its short commit, and a run that has not pinned one yet shows a dash (P8-89, UX-17).
- Given a run in the history, when I open its "More actions" menu, then it offers Zip and Delete; Delete asks for the confirmation of RF-1012 and returns the focus to the menu button when the dialog closes; for a run that is queued or running both are disabled with the reason. A run that can be resumed also shows its Resume button beside the menu (RF-006). No red button stands in the table (P8-89, UX-17, EV-94).
  Verification: end-to-end test.

**RF-1009 Protect every request** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-46, DEC-62, DEC-69 - ADR-013, ADR-015, ADR-017
As a user I want every page and API call to require my session so that no one else can drive Rosetta or read my results.

- Given any route other than the landing page, sign-in, `/healthz`, static assets and `robots.txt`, when it is requested without a valid session, then pages redirect to sign-in and the API answers 401.
- Given a state-changing request (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE), when its `Origin` differs from the configured public URL or its CSRF token is missing or wrong, then it is refused with 403 and a security event is logged. The sign-in request, which has no session yet, is protected by the `Origin` check and the `SameSite=Strict` cookie (DEC-112).
- Given any response, when it is sent, then it never contains a password hash, a session id other than in the cookie, a provider key or another secret.
- Given a GET or HEAD request for a page path outside `/api/` and `/assets/`, when no route matches, then the server answers `index.html` (the app shell) so that the client router can show the page; `/sign-in` is public, and every other page follows the session rules above (DEC-120).
- Given the local Compose deployment, when it starts, then the app port is published on `127.0.0.1` only (RF-1301).
  Verification: integration tests for each refused request and for the app shell fallback; output scan test.

**RF-1010 Project tokens and cost always visible** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-49 - ADR-007, ADR-012
As a developer I want the tokens and price spent on the whole project in view at all times so that I never lose track of what the analysis costs.

- Given any page of the web UI, when it is open, then a header bar shows the project's total tokens (input, output, cached) and total cost from the cost ledger (RF-428), with a breakdown by run, stage, provider and model one click away.
- Given a run in progress, when a model call completes, then the project totals in the header update live together with the run's own meter (RF-1003).
- Given a run in progress and a window 1280 px wide or wider, when the header shows the run meter, then the header is one line: the wordmark, the run meter (after the wordmark, as the artboard `P8Projects` draws it), the totals, the user's name with the role, Sign out and the theme toggle share one row, and no text of them breaks over two lines; this holds for the administrator as well (P8-101, UX-18).
- Given a run in progress and a window from 1024 px to 1279 px wide, when the header shows the run meter, then the totals, the user's name with the role, Sign out and the theme toggle stay on the first line and the run meter takes a line of its own under the wordmark; no text of the header breaks over two lines and the page does not scroll sideways (P8-101, UX-18).
- Given a run in progress, when the window is 390 px or 360 px wide, then the header may use more than one line and the user's name, Sign out and the theme toggle may wrap among themselves, but nothing is cut off, "Sign out" and the theme toggle never break inside a word, and the page does not scroll sideways, at 390 px and at 360 px, with or without the run meter (P8-101, UX-18).
- Given a user whose display name is long (up to 200 characters, RF-1102), when the header shows it, then the name is cut with an ellipsis and its full text is the tooltip of the name; the cut name never moves Sign out or the theme toggle off the row, they never break inside a word, and no page scrolls sideways at any width from 360 px to 1440 px (P8-101).
- Given no run in progress and a window 1280 px or wider, when the totals are shown, then the total figures and the split into input, output and cached tokens stand on one line of the pill and the header is no taller than the pill's one line makes it (about 66 px), as on every page before P8-101, even when the totals are large (up to 999,999,999 tokens a kind and $9,999.99).
- Given a window from 1024 px to 1279 px, or a run in progress and a window 1024 px or wider, when the header shows the totals, then the pill shows the total figures on one line and the split into input, output and cached tokens is the tooltip of the figures; the header is one line (two with the run meter at 1024 px to 1279 px) however large the totals are (P8-101).
- Given the administrator and a window narrower than 1600 px, when the header shows the totals, then the server's spend this month against its monthly cap is not in the header, with a run or without; it stays on the users and caps page and in the cap banner from 80% of the cap (RF-1106), and the header is one line from 1280 px (P8-101).
- Given no run in progress, when the page is opened after runs have finished, then the same totals are shown from the ledger; the report dashboard of each run also shows the project totals at the time it was generated.
- Given my account, when the header is shown, then totals cover my own projects only; the administrator also sees the server totals for the month against the server cap, in the header from 1600 px and on the users and caps page otherwise (RF-1106, P8-101).
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events and a fixture ledger.

**RF-1011 Live provider calls and logs view** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-50 - ADR-012
As a developer I want to watch the calls to the model providers and Rosetta's log in the page so that I can spot slow, failing or expensive calls while a run works.

- Given a run, when calls are made, then an "API calls" panel lists each call live from the provider call log (RF-408) with provider, model, agent, latency, tokens, cost and status, filterable by provider, role, agent and status, with errors and retries highlighted, the provider's error text beside a failed call (RF-408) and a link to the masked transcript.
- Given a run with hundreds of calls, when the panel opens, then it shows the first page of calls (100) with the summary and the total of the whole log, a "Load more calls" button loads the next page, the calls the run makes meanwhile appear without a reload, and a call's transcript opens in well under a second once the log has been read (P5-22). The route `GET /api/runs/:runId/calls` takes `limit` (at most 500) and `cursor` and answers `nextCursor` while more calls follow.
- Given the page, when it is open, then summary figures per provider and role show calls, errors, P50 and P95 latency, P50 and P95 time to first token, and tokens per second for Ollama (DEC-57).
- Given security events in the log, when the page is open, then a counter shows how many occurred in the run, and clicking it filters the log to them.
- Given the application log (RF-009), when I open the "Logs" panel, then recent entries stream live with a level filter.
  Verification: end-to-end test with recorded events.

**RF-1012 Delete snapshots and runs from the page** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-51, DEC-55 - ADR-012
As a user I want to delete my old runs and projects from the page so that my workspace does not grow without limit; the administrator also deletes unused snapshots.

- Given my history, or for the administrator the snapshot list, when I press Delete on a run, a project or a snapshot, then a confirmation names what will be deleted and the disk space it frees, and only after I confirm is the folder removed and an `info` entry written to the application log.
- Given a snapshot used by a run in progress, or a run in progress, when I try to delete it, then the button is disabled with the reason.
- Given any deletion, when it completes, then the `cost_ledger` rows are kept and the totals (RF-1010) still include the deleted runs' calls.
  Verification: end-to-end test on a temporary project; integration test that the ledger is untouched.

**RF-1013 Security headers and cookies** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-57, DEC-69 - ADR-013, ADR-015, ADR-017
As a user I want the application hardened so that its session never leaks and injected content cannot run.

- Given any response, when it is sent, then it carries `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'self'; connect-src 'self'; img-src 'self' data:; style-src 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'self'`, `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`; when served over HTTPS it also carries `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`.
- Given a session, when its cookie is set, then it is `__Host-rosetta_session` with `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict` and `Path=/`, and no session id or token ever appears in a URL.
- Given the static report, when it is generated, then each page carries an equivalent content security policy in a `meta` element and `referrer` set to `no-referrer`.
  Verification: integration tests on response headers and cookie attributes.

**RF-1014 Honest verification state** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-136 - ADR-005
As a user I want the verification state to say what was checked and what was not so that a claim is never shown as waiting for a step that does not run.

- Given a release in which only the citation check runs (the model judgement of RF-301 is not built yet), when a claim passed its citation check, then it is shown as "citations checked" with a short note that the judgement step comes in a later version, and the stepper and the run summary say the same; nothing is labelled "waiting" for a step that will not run.
- Given the judgement step is built, when a claim is waiting for it, then "waiting for judgement" is shown only while that step is queued or running.
  Verification: unit test of the labels; end-to-end test of the live run page.

**RF-1015 Run page in two modes** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-137, DEC-142 - ADR-012
As a user who may not know the analysed code I want the run page to show one thing at a time so that I always know what is happening and what to do next.

- Given a queued or running run, when its page opens, then it is in "while running" mode: the run journey (map the code, read, check citations, explain, your questions) with the current step, the living code map (RF-1016), the spend for the run against its cap, and up to three "working now" agents in plain words; cards so far, all agents, and activity (events, provider calls and logs) sit in tabs below, one list at a time.
- Given a plan run (Q-44), when its page opens, then its stepper shows the plan steps (brief, planner calls, package, checks), never the understand steps; no code map, retry notice or Deepen notice is shown on a plan run.
- Given a plan run with a planner call in progress (package, a document, a gap round or a repair), when its page is open, then the current step "Planner calls" shows that the planner is working: a moving indicator, the call's name, the time since it started and the tokens so far, updated live; between calls the step says which call comes next (P7-25).
- Given a plan run, running or ended, when its page opens, then it shows plan figures only: no Agents, Cards or Claims panel and no cards, citations or areas figures; it lists the planner calls with their tokens and cost, and once the package is written says where its checks are (`checks.md`) (P7-25).
- Given a plan run that failed or stopped, when the "Your turn" panel opens, then it names the plan's own next action, "Try the plan again", and for a package written with gaps (RST-1806) says that `checks.md` lists the items no task card builds; it never offers "Resume run" or says that "Set up the plan" appears once the run completes, which belong to an understand run (P7-25).
- Given a run that has ended, when its page opens, then it is in "after the run" mode: the overview (RF-264) first, a "Your turn" panel with the number of open questions and the next actions, followed by the journey to the package (RF-615), and tabs for Your questions, Evidence (cards, claims, citations, coverage, code map) and Activity (replay, events, calls, logs); the page switches modes by itself when the run ends.
- Given a finished understand run that can be deepened, when its page opens, then the journey strip and "Your turn" together offer one Deepen button: the strip points to the Deepen of "Your turn" in words and has no button of its own (P8-11, EV-90).
- Given a plan run that failed or stopped, when "Try the plan again" is used, then Plan setup opens for the understand run the plan was made from; the options call of Plan setup, when refused for the private code confirmation (RST-1521), says to tick the confirmation on Plan setup (P8-11, EV-86).
- Given the checks of a written package, when the page asks for them, then the answer carries at most 64 KiB of text and says when it was cut (P8-11).
- Given "Retry failed areas" on a run that recorded no cloud consent, when the failed areas use a cloud provider, then the consent box shows from the start beside the button, and the error of a failed estimate shows beside the button (P8-11, P7-38).
- Given a run page of any kind and state, when it opens, then its title names the run and its state ("Scan, done", "Understand, running", "Deepen round 2, done") and never "Live run" once the run has ended; the run's folder name is a line under it (P8-27, UX-11).
- Given a scan run, when its page opens, then it shows the code map and the downloads that apply to a scan, and no stepper step of understand or verify, no "Your turn" panel, no report and no hand-off download; a download that cannot be used yet says why in visible text, and one that can never apply to the run kind is not drawn (P8-27, UX-11, UX-12).
- Given an ended run page, when it shows the figures of the run (supported claims of those judged, cards, areas, cost, tokens), then each figure is shown once, from one count; a count that is the journey's total says "of the journey", and the figures list has no empty cell at any width (P8-27, UX-13, UX-22).
- Given an area with no file to read, when the run plans its readers, then no reader task is planned for it; and a tile of the code map wraps its text instead of clipping it (P8-27, UX-14).
- Given the page of a run opened by its address, when the side menu is drawn, then it shows the pages of the run's project, as on any other page of the project (P8-27, UX-26).
- Given a finished plan run whose journey offers "Open the package", when it is used on the page of that run, then the page scrolls to the package section, which holds the downloads and the summary of `checks.md`, and moves the focus to it (P8-27).
- Given either mode, when it is used with the keyboard or a screen reader, then tabs follow the tab pattern, every state is carried by text and a glyph as well as colour, and RF-1002..RF-1005, RF-1007, RF-1010 and RF-1011 keep their content in these places.
  Verification: end-to-end tests of both modes and the switch; accessibility checks; the design proposal `docs/design/proposals/run-page-v2.html` is the reference.

**RF-1016 Living code map** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-137 - ADR-012
As a user watching a run I want to see the code being read so that the run is easy to follow and pleasant to watch.

- Given the code map of the run, when the run page of an understand run is in "while running" mode, then each area is a tile sized by its estimated tokens, showing its name, file count and state (waiting, being read with a moving highlight, read with one mark per card written, not covered, or third-party code left out, drawn hatched); a tile opens its agent and cards.
- Given the operating system asks for reduced motion, when the map updates, then nothing moves; states change in place.
  Verification: component tests of the tile states; end-to-end test with recorded events; reduced-motion check.

**RF-1017 Overview and questions after the run** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-137, DEC-142, DEC-144, DEC-178, DEC-179 - ADR-012
As a user I want the end of a run to read like an explanation followed by a short request for help so that I understand the application before I answer anything.

- Given an ended run, when its page opens, then the Overview tab shows the overview of RF-264 with its sections (features, screens, data, integrations), each item linking its cards, the honest verification note of RF-1014 and what was not covered.
- Given open questions (RF-243), when the Your questions tab opens, then each shows the question, why the code cannot answer it with its citation, how many items depend on it, an answer field and the default that applies if it is skipped, which is the recommended suggested answer (RF-645, DEC-178): the suggested answers are shown as choices with the recommended one first, plus "Type your own answer".
- Given a run that ended `Completed`, when the "Your turn" panel lists the next actions, then they include "Set up the plan" (RF-614) and Deepen with its estimate (RF-209).
- Given a run that can be deepened, when the "Your turn" panel lists the next actions, then it recommends Deepen before answering and says that answers given now will be used by it; while a Deepen is queued or running it says that a Deepen is running and the questions are asked after it, and does not recommend Deepen again; once the last Deepen has ended, or when the user chose not to Deepen, it asks the user to answer the questions still open (RF-646, DEC-179).
- Given an ended run with questions, when the "Your turn" panel and the Your questions tab show how many questions there are, then both count the questions that wait for an answer, and a panel with none waiting says how many have a saved answer in words that do not contradict the tab (P8-27, UX-22).
  Verification: end-to-end test with a recorded run.
  Changed 2026-11-09 by DEC-211 (P8-84): "Your turn" recommends Deepen before answering only while the run read its own code below the coverage bar; otherwise Set up the plan is its one primary button and Deepen a link (RF-1021).

**RF-1018 Choose areas quickly** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-138 - ADR-012
As a user on the start page I want to choose the areas of a run in a few clicks so that I can start the run without scrolling through a long list.

- Given the list of areas, when the start page opens, then only the project's own code is listed and selected; third-party and generated code is left out and shown as one collapsed line after the list ("12 library areas left out: jQuery, Bootstrap, ... Show"), which expands to the areas with their reason and lets the user add any of them; buttons select all, select none and select own code only, and a filter narrows the list by name.
- Given any number of areas, when the page is scrolled, then a summary bar stays visible at the bottom with the number of areas and files chosen, the estimate and the Start button; the list itself scrolls inside its panel.
- Given a user who selects third-party areas, when they start the run, then the estimate shows what they add and the start asks for confirmation once.
  Verification: end-to-end test with 1 and 120 areas at desktop and phone widths.

**RF-1019 Starting a step opens its live page** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-204 - ADR-012, ADR-027
As a user I want a started step to show itself so that I never click "Open the live run".

- Given any journey step that starts (understand, Deepen, the plan), when the start is accepted, then the browser opens that run's live page by itself.
- Given a step that is queued behind other runs (RF-1108), when its page opens, then it says that it is waiting, its place in the queue, and keeps the journey strip (RF-1020).
  Verification: web tests of each start; an end-to-end test of the start.

**RF-1020 The journey strip on the project page and every run page** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-204 - ADR-012, ADR-027
As a user I want to see where I am in the journey and what each step cost so that I always know the next move.

- Given the project page, or any other page once its step details are shown (P8-100), of a project with a journey, when it opens, then the journey strip shows each step with its state (waiting, running, waiting for approval, done, skipped, failed) and its cost, the current step is marked, and each step is a link to its page.
- Given the strip, when it is used with the keyboard or a screen reader, then it is an ordered list, the current step has `aria-current="step"`, and every state is carried by a word and a glyph as well as colour.
- Given an understand or plan run that I stopped with Stop, when the strip shows its step, then the step's state word is "Stopped by you", not "Failed", and the project title reads "Understand stopped by you" or "Plan stopped by you" (P8-87).
- Given an understand or plan run that a restart of the server stopped, or that was cancelled while queued, when the strip shows its step, then the step's state word is "Stopped by a restart" or "Cancelled while queued" in the same warn tone and pause glyph as "Stopped by you", never "Failed" and never "Stopped by you"; the project title reads "Understand stopped by a restart", "Understand cancelled while queued", "Plan stopped by a restart" or "Plan cancelled while queued"; and the step still offers Resume or "Try the plan again" (P8-111, DEC-217).
- Given an understand or plan run that ended `CapReached`, when the strip shows its step, then the step's state word is "Stopped at its cap" in the warn tone with a warning glyph, never "Failed"; the detail keeps the run's code, or "Partly read" and the code when planned areas were left unread (P7-02); the project title reads "Understand stopped at its cap" or "Plan stopped at its cap" in the warn tone; and the step still offers its next action, Resume for an understand run and "Try the plan again" for a plan run (DEC-217, P8-112).
- Given any page that shows the strip except the project page (a scan, understand or plan run page, Plan setup), when it opens, then the strip is one compact row of the seven steps in order, each with its glyph, its name and its state word, the current step marked with `aria-current="step"` and the text "Current step"; the reason or code, the cost and the links of a step are not shown in the row, and the next action, the total so far and the Past runs and settings links stay under it (P8-100, UX-05, UX-15, UX-18, UX-20).
- Given the compact row, when I choose its "Show step details" button, then the detail cards of the seven steps appear with each step's reason or code, cost and links, the button reads "Hide step details" and says in `aria-expanded` which it is, and choosing it again returns to the row; the button is reached and used with the keyboard, and a step that is a link stays a link in the row (P8-100, UX-15).
- Given the project page, when it opens, then the strip is expanded as before, shows the detail cards and offers no show or hide button (P8-100).
- Given a journey with an understand run, a Deepen and a plan run, and a Continue that raised its approved amount, when the strip shows the total so far and the approved line, then the total is the spend of every understand, Deepen and plan run of the journey (the journey API's `budget.spentMicros`; the Cost page's project total also counts scans and the plan-options calls, which are no step of the journey, so it can be higher, P9-14), a run queued before the journey began is not counted, and the line reads what is left of the approved amount including every Continue (P9-08, EV-107, RF-011).
- Given the compact row at 1280 px, 1024 px and 360 px, when it is shown, then nothing scrolls sideways and the states stay words and glyphs, never colour alone (P8-100, RNF-010).
  Verification: component and web tests of the strip states; an accessibility check.

**RF-1021 The page offers the next step in place** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-179, DEC-204 - ADR-012, ADR-027
As a user I want the page to offer the next step when a step ends so that I do not go back to a start page.

- Given a step that ended, when its page is shown, then a next-step panel offers the next action in place, with its estimate and what is left of the approved amount: "Deepen" with what it would read, "Answer questions" when the run has open questions (RF-647), "Set up the plan", then "Start the plan".
- Given a finished understand, when the panel is shown, then Deepen is a button and never starts by itself, and the plan is a button that never starts by itself after the plan setup (DEC-204); the order of Deepen and the questions follows RF-646 and RF-1017.
- Given a finished understand that can be deepened (it ended `Completed`, no Deepen of it was made or is queued or running, and the viewer did not choose to skip it, RF-646) and that read an area of own code below the coverage bar (the "Partly read" of RF-230), when "Your turn" is shown, then it is headed "Your turn: read the rest of the code", says what the readers covered ("The readers covered 8,702 of 52,749 tokens of your own code. A Deepen reads the 21 files never read and re-checks the 23 rejected claims. Answers you give before it are used by it.") and offers "Deepen this run" as its one primary button; under it are the other paths as secondary links: "Answer the 3 questions first" while questions wait (it opens them and the Deepen stays the next step) and "Skip the Deepen and set up the plan" when the run has a Supported claim (it records the choice not to Deepen, RF-646, and opens Plan setup for the run) (P8-84, UX-24, UX-25).
- Given a finished understand with a Supported claim that is not in the case above (every area of own code read at the bar or above, its coverage not known, a Deepen of it made or running, or its Deepen skipped), when "Your turn" is shown, then it is headed "Your turn: set up the plan" and "Set up the plan" is its one primary button; the other paths are secondary links under it: "Answer the 3 questions first" while questions wait and "Deepen this run" while the run can be deepened and no Deepen of it is queued or running. While a Deepen of it is queued or running, the panel also says so as RF-1017 asks. Outside the case above, a panel with no plan to set up (a run that failed or was interrupted, or that has no Supported claim) has no primary button (P8-84, UX-24, UX-25).
- Given the page of an understand run that has ended, when it is shown, then the page as a whole (its head, the journey strip and "Your turn") has one primary action: for a run that completed with a Supported claim it is the primary button of "Your turn" above; for a run that stopped at its cap, failed or was interrupted it is Resume in the head of the page (RF-006), and "Your turn" offers "Set up the plan" (a run at its cap with a Supported claim, P3-121) and "Deepen this run" (a run at its cap that left areas unread, DEC-194) only as links; for a run with no Supported claim it is the strip's "Start a new run". Everywhere else the strip draws its next action as a secondary button, and "Retry failed areas" (RF-208) is secondary. The strip reads the coverage as "Your turn" does, so it recommends Deepen first exactly while "Your turn" does (also with no question open or a brief saved) and never says "Deepen first" while "Your turn" offers the plan; the project page, which does not read the coverage, keeps the rule of RF-646 (P8-84).
- Given any journey page, when it is shown, then Past runs and the project settings are one click away (links under the strip).
- Given a project with an approved journey amount (RF-435), when the strip is shown, then it says what is left of that amount, for example "$0.60 left of $1.00 approved"; a project with no journey record shows no amount.
- Given the project page, a scan run page and an understand run page that is still working, when they open, then each shows the strip (RF-1020); Deepen is offered on the project page with its estimate shown at once and starts only on the click and the confirmation (DEC-204).
- Given a finished understand, when I go on, then Deepen, the questions, the plan setup and the plan are each reached from the page they follow, with no visit to another page.
- Given a project that has a run, when its page opens, then the page is titled with the project name and the current step of the journey ("Package ready", "Questions waiting"), and "Start a run" is the title only before the first run (P8-26, DEC-211).
- Given a project with a written package, when its page opens, then it shows the package state (the hand-off package with its downloads, the journey cost against the approved amount, the questions left on their defaults, the runs of the project) and no upload form; a new version of the source is uploaded from a collapsed "Replace the source" section, and the summary bar never covers a control: the page reserves its height at the bottom.
- Given a message that points to a control (the cloud notice or the private code notice that unlocks Analyse), when it is shown, then it says where the control is in the order of the page and offers to go to it.
- Given questions with an answer that wait for a re-run, when the project page is shown, then the choice to re-run with them is a labelled control in the Questions step of the strip, and the strip counts the same questions (it never says "No open questions" while an answered question waits).
