- Given a run of the project that is queued or running, when the project page is shown, then the strip step that waits for it is a link to that run's page, and the title says that a run is live.
  Verification: web tests of the panel for each state; an end-to-end test from a finished understand to a downloaded package.

**RF-1022 The start page has no stage and no caps** - R1 - Must - Source DEC-204 - ADR-012, ADR-027
As a user I want a start page without technical choices so that I do not decide stages or caps.

- Given the start page, when it opens, then it holds no stage choice and no per-run caps, models or parallelism; they are removed, not hidden behind an option (DEC-204).
- Given the old start-page address, when I open it, then I am redirected to the project page. The start page itself is removed and this redirect is all that remains of it (P7-37, P8-15 closing the P7-33 note).
- Given the areas, when the journey is estimated, then the project's own code is read as in RF-1018, and the choice of areas stays on the project page before Analyse.
- Given the estimate of the start page, when it is shown, then the journey figures are three short lines in the words of the Deepen panel (RF-434): "Likely", "Approved" (what Analyse approves, with "likely above what can be approved" when DEC-210 applies) and "What stops it"; the explanatory sentences are collapsed under them (P8-26).
- Given a model or an area name in the estimate or the areas list, when the line wraps, then it breaks only at its separators (" / ", "/"), never inside a name (P8-26).
- Given a project that has a run whose journey does not start again, when its page is shown, then the areas, the estimate and Analyse are under a collapsed "Start another run"; they are open when the journey has to start (no run, or a run with nothing to plan from).
  Verification: web tests of the page and the redirect; an end-to-end test of the journey from the project page.

### 5.10 Accounts, sessions, keys and administration (RF-1100..RF-1199)

**RF-1100 Sign in with a user name and password** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-62 - ADR-015
As a user I want to sign in with my user name and password so that only invited people use Rosetta.

- Given the sign-in page, when I enter a correct user name and password for an enabled account, then a session starts (RF-1101) and I land on my projects.
- Given a wrong user name or password, when I submit, then the page says only "User name or password is incorrect", with the same response time whether or not the user exists.
- Given five failed attempts for one user name within 15 minutes, when another attempt arrives, then the account is locked for 15 minutes, the attempt is refused with the same message, and a security event is logged; sign-in is also rate limited to 10 attempts per minute per IP address.
- Given I submit a wrong current password on the change-password form (RF-1103), when it is refused, then the attempt counts toward the same lockout as a failed sign-in (DEC-119).
- Given any password, when it is stored, then only its scrypt hash with salt and parameters is kept (ADR-015).
  Verification: integration tests for success, failure, lockout, rate limit and timing; unit test on the hash format.

**RF-1101 Sessions and sign-out** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-62 - ADR-015
As a user I want my session to be safe and to end when I leave so that a forgotten browser does not stay signed in.

- Given a successful sign-in, when the session is created, then its id is new (any previous id is discarded), random (256 bits), only its SHA-256 is stored, and the cookie follows RF-1013.
- Given 8 hours without activity or 7 days since sign-in, when the next request arrives, then the session is ended and I must sign in again.
- Given Sign out, when I press it, then the session is deleted on the server and the cookie is cleared; sign-out is a single button in the page header (DEC-127).
- Given an administrator disables my account or resets my password, when my next request arrives, then all my sessions are ended.
  Verification: integration tests with a fixed clock.

**RF-1102 Administer accounts** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-62, DEC-66 - ADR-015
As the administrator I want to create and manage accounts so that I decide who uses Rosetta.

- Given the first start of an empty database, when `ROSETTA_ADMIN_USER` and `ROSETTA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` are set, then that administrator account is created once and the password must be changed at first sign-in; the values are never logged. A `ROSETTA_ADMIN_PASSWORD` that breaks the RF-1103 password rules stops start-up (DEC-119).
- Given the administration page, when I create an account, then I set the user name, display name, role (`admin` or `user`), an initial password and whether it must be changed at first sign-in (default on; off for the teacher account, RF-1107); there is no self sign-up anywhere.
- Given an account, when I disable it, enable it or reset its password, then the change takes effect at once (RF-1101) and an audit event is written (RF-1109).
- Given my own account, when I try to disable it or reset its password from the administration page, then it is refused with RST-2213 (an administrator cannot disable or reset their own account; the own password is changed with RF-1103) (DEC-119).
- Given the administration page, when I open it, then I see every account with its last sign-in, number of projects and month-to-date cost.
  Verification: integration tests per action; end-to-end test of the page; authorization test that a `user` gets 403.

**RF-1103 Change my password** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-62 - ADR-015
As a user I want to change my password so that only I know it.

- Given my settings, when I enter my current password and a new one, then the new one is accepted only if it has at least 12 characters, is not on the common-passwords list and does not contain my user name; my other sessions are ended.
- Given a new password equal to my current one, when I submit, then it is refused with RST-2214 (DEC-119).
- Given a wrong current password, when I submit, then the change is refused and the attempt counts toward the sign-in lockout (RF-1100, DEC-119).
  Verification: integration tests for each rule.

**RF-1104 My provider API keys** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-64 - ADR-015
As a user I want to store my own API keys for the providers so that runs use my account; otherwise the server's keys are used under caps.

- Given my settings, when I save a key for a provider (OpenAI, Anthropic, an OpenAI-compatible endpoint), then it is encrypted (AES-256-GCM with the server secret) and stored; the page shows only its last four characters, and no API ever returns it.
- Given a run, when a role needs a provider, then my key for it is used if I have one; otherwise the server key is used if the administrator allows server keys for my account (default: allowed), and the ledger records which (RF-428).
- Given I replace or delete a key, when I confirm, then the old value is gone from the database and an audit event is written without the value.
- Given Ollama in the local deployment, when it is configured, then no key is needed; the hosted deployment offers Ollama only if the operator sets `ROSETTA_OLLAMA_URL` (DEC-112).
- Given an OpenAI-compatible key with a base URL, when it is saved, then the URL must use `https` and must not resolve to a private, loopback or link-local address, checked when it is saved and again when each call connects; otherwise it is refused with an error code. This key row is the only place a base URL is set; project settings never set one (DEC-112).
- Given a key to save, when its length is outside 16 to 512 characters, or its base URL carries a query string (an Azure `api-version` URL is refused), or its host does not resolve within 5 seconds, then it is refused with an error code (DEC-123).
  Verification: integration tests on storage, use and deletion; output scan test that no response contains a key.

**RF-1105 Private workspaces** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-66 - ADR-015, ADR-016
As a user I want my projects and runs to be visible to me only so that my work stays private.

- Given any list, page, event stream, file download or API call, when I request it, then it includes only my projects, runs, events, files and ledger rows; another user's id in a URL answers 404.
- Given the administrator, when they open another user's workspace from the administration page, then they can read it, and an audit event is written.
  Verification: integration tests with two users for every route (access matrix test).

**RF-1106 Usage caps** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-13, DEC-64 - ADR-007, ADR-015
As the administrator I want limits on what users spend with the server keys so that the bill stays under control.

- Given server keys, when any call would push one of these over its cap, then the call is refused before it is made and the run ends with `CapReached`: the run cap (default $0.50), the user's daily cap (default $1.00) and the server's monthly cap (default $10.00), all configurable on the administration page.
- Given server keys, when a run's cap is set or raised, then the effective run cap is the lower of the run's cap and the administrator's run cap; "Raise cap" stops at the administrator's cap (DEC-107).
- Given a user's own keys, when they run, then only the run cap and the user's own daily cap (optional, set in their settings, default none) apply; their spend does not count against the server cap.
- Given price overrides in a project's settings, when a call uses a server key, then the shipped price table is used and the overrides apply only to calls with the user's own keys (DEC-107).
- Given the daily and monthly caps, when spend is summed, then the periods are UTC calendar days and months (DEC-107).
- Given the server reaches 80% of its monthly cap, when the next page loads for the administrator, then a warning banner is shown.
- Given server keys and an administrator run cap lower than the project run cap, when a run starts, then `run.started` records the administrator run cap as the effective run cap with `source` `administrator`, the run page meter is held to it, and the start page names it (P3-91).
- Given own keys only, when a run starts, then `run.started` records the project run cap with `source` `project` and no administrator figure, and no administrator line is shown (P3-91).
- Given server keys and a daily cap set by the administrator for the account, when a run starts, then `run.started` records that day cap with `source` `account`; without one it records the server default with `source` `administrator` (P3-91).
- Given a call refused by a cap, when the run reports it, then `budget.capReached` carries `capSource` (`project`, `administrator` or `account`) when the cap has a source, and the live log names the cap reached and who can raise it (P3-91).
- Given a project whose run cap is typed above the administrator run cap, when the project settings show the caps, then a note says that a run on server keys stops at the administrator run cap; a note is shown whenever the administrator run cap is lower than the run cap typed in the settings, whether or not a server key will be used, and no note is shown when it is not lower (P3-91).
  Verification: integration tests per cap with a fixed clock; unit tests of the start caps, of the `run.started` and `budget.capReached` events, of the cap text and of the start-page and settings notes (P3-91).

**RF-1107 Teacher account** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-62, DEC-67 - ADR-015
As the product owner I want an account for the evaluators so that they can try Rosetta on the hosted URL.

- Given the hosted deployment, when the administrator creates the account `teacher` with role `user`, then its password is set by the administrator outside the repository, it does not need a change at first sign-in, and it is delivered only in the hand-in form (RF-803).
- Given the teacher account, when it signs in, then it sees an empty workspace (Q-21) and can run the demo with the server keys under the caps.
  Verification: manual sign-in on the hosted URL.

**RF-1108 Run queue** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-61 - ADR-014
As a user I want my run to wait its turn when the server is busy so that the server stays responsive.

- Given more running runs than the limit (a constant, 2; it is not a setting, DEC-122), when I start a run, then it is queued, the page shows its position, and it starts automatically when a slot frees; one user has at most one running run.
- Given the server restarts while runs are running or queued, when it starts again, then running runs end as `Interrupted` (resumable with RF-006) and queued runs stay queued in their order (DEC-112).
- Given a queued run, when I open any page that shows it (the start page, the Projects page, the live run page, the run history and the journey), then the page shows its position and how many runs are ahead of it, and the live run page keeps that number current while the run waits (EV-80, P7-22). A wait estimate is not part of this requirement.
- Given a queued paid run (an understand, plan or Deepen run) and a paid run of the same user that is running, when I open the live run page of the queued run, then the page says that it waits for my running run to end and links that run by its id, beside its position (EV-106, P9-10); a queued scan never waits for my paid run, and a run that waits only for a free slot keeps the position alone.
- Given a scan (it calls no model) and a run of the same user that is running, when the scan is queued, then it starts at once if a slot is free, and it never waits behind the user's paid run; a running scan does not hold back the user's next paid run (EV-91, P8-10).
- Given a run resumed (RF-006) or continued (RF-432) that was queued before the journey now recorded (P9-01, P9-16), when it is queued again, then it keeps its queue time and so goes ahead of the runs queued after that time, which is where it stood; it does not become a step of the newer journey. Any other resumed or continued run is queued at the end of the queue, and a new run is always queued at the end. The queue order is the queue time, then the run id.
- Given a Deepen or a plan run that names its source (DEC-226), when it is queued, then its row records the run it improves or is written from, so the journey places it, with its position in the queue, before it starts and without reading its manifest.
  Verification: integration test with the fake provider; integration test of the row written at queue time (P8-139).

**RF-1109 Security audit trail** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-69, DEC-124, DEC-132, DEC-141 - ADR-013, ADR-015
As the administrator I want account and key events recorded so that I can see who did what.

- Given a sign-in (success or failure), lockout, sign-out, account change, key change, administrator access to another workspace, or a refused request, when it happens, then an audit row with time, actor, action, target, IP address and outcome is stored and a `warn` or `info` log entry is written (RF-009); no row contains a password or key. Refused requests write `request.refused` rows up to 30 a minute per client address; past that, one `request.refused` row with reason `rate-limit` counts the rest of that minute, and the requests are still refused with their usual status. The stored path is cut to 512 characters with a marker (DEC-124, DEC-141).
- Given the administration page, when I open the audit view, then I can filter the last 30 days by user and action.
- Given audit rows older than 365 days, when the daily retention task runs, then they are deleted and rows from the last 365 days are kept; the server role can delete no audit row younger than 365 days (DEC-132).
  Verification: integration tests per event; a retention test on PostgreSQL.

### 5.11 Landing page (RF-1200..RF-1299)

**RF-1200 Landing page** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-63 - ADR-012
As a visitor I want a page that explains Rosetta well so that I understand what it does before signing in.

- Given the root URL, when it is opened without a session, then a product-quality landing page shows: what Rosetta does in one sentence; the pipeline (a GitHub URL, a zip or Excel workbooks, then scan, agents, verifier, report, hand-off; sources changed by DEC-153) as a graphic; the live agent view; evidence-cited cards with the verifier; cost control; security and privacy; the technology; who built it and why (a master's final project); and a Sign in button.
- Given a signed-in user, when they open the root URL, then they go to their projects.
- Given any screen width from 360 px, when the page is shown, then it follows the design system (RNF-010) and works without JavaScript.
- Given the six stages of the pipeline ("From a GitHub URL, a zip or Excel workbooks to a hand-off your team can build from"), when the page is shown at a width above 600 px, then the stages lie out in rows of 6, of 3 + 3 or of 2 + 2 + 2, never 5 + 1 or any other split that leaves a stage alone or a row shorter than the others, whatever the width: 6 in one row at 1280 px, 3 + 3 at 1024 px and at 768 px (P8-102, UX-20).
- Given the six stages, when the page is shown at 600 px or less, then they are one column, a vertical rail as the artboard draws it, in the order of the pipeline.
  Verification: end-to-end test with axe; end-to-end test of the rows of the six stages at 1280, 1100, 1024, 768, 601 and 390 px, with a screenshot at 1280 and 390 px; review against the design board.

**RF-1201 Landing page reach** - R1 (M1) - Should - Source DEC-63, Q-19, Q-20 - ADR-014
As the product owner I want the landing page seen only by the people I share the URL with so that the project is not public yet.

- Given any page of the application, when it is served, then it carries `noindex, nofollow`, and `robots.txt` disallows everything.
- Given a contact address in the server configuration, when the landing page is shown, then a "Request access" link opens an e-mail to it; without the setting there is no such link and no sign-up.
  Verification: integration tests on headers and `robots.txt`.

### 5.12 Container, local and hosted deployment (RF-1300..RF-1399)

**RF-1300 One container image** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-61 - ADR-014
As the product owner I want one image for every deployment so that what I test is what the teachers use.

- Given the repository, when the image is built, then a multi-stage `Dockerfile` produces a runtime image from a Node.js 24 slim base pinned by digest, with the built server and front end, the migrations and the prompts, running as a non-root user, listening on port 8080, with a `HEALTHCHECK` on `/healthz`.
- Given the image, when it is inspected, then it contains no secret, no `.env`, no source maps of server code, no test files and no build tools.
  Verification: CI builds the image and runs a container scan; an integration test starts it and calls `/healthz`.

**RF-1301 Local deployment with Docker Compose** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-61 - ADR-014, ADR-016
As the product owner I want to run the whole system on my machine exactly as it will run hosted so that I can test the deployment.

- Given `compose.yaml` and a `.env` copied from `.env.example`, when I run `docker compose up`, then PostgreSQL and the app start with named volumes for the database and the data folder, migrations run, and the app is reachable at `http://127.0.0.1:8080` only.
- Given Ollama running on the host, when a role uses it, then the app reaches it at `http://host.docker.internal:11434`.
- Given `docker compose down` and `up` again, when the app starts, then accounts, projects, runs and snapshots are still there.
  Verification: manual run on the owner's machine; a CI job runs the Compose stack and an end-to-end smoke test with the fake provider.

**RF-1302 Database migrations at start-up** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-65 - ADR-016
As the product owner I want the database schema updated automatically and safely so that a new image never runs on an old schema.

- Given numbered migration files, when the app starts, then pending migrations are applied in order, each in a transaction, and recorded with their checksum in `schema_version` before the server accepts requests.
- Given an applied migration whose file has changed, or a database with a migration the image does not know, when the app starts, then it stops with an error code and does not serve requests.
  Verification: integration tests on a disposable database.

**RF-1303 Health endpoint** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-61 - ADR-014

- Given `GET /healthz`, when the server and the database are reachable, then it answers 200 with `{ "status": "ok", "version": "<rosetta version>" }`; otherwise 503 with no detail; it needs no session and reveals no secret.
  Verification: integration test.

**RF-1304 Hosted deployment** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-61, DEC-67 - ADR-014
As the product owner I want Rosetta running on a server so that my teachers can use it from anywhere.

- Given the host chosen in Q-18, when the image is deployed, then it runs behind the host's HTTPS with managed PostgreSQL, a persistent disk mounted at `ROSETTA_DATA_DIR`, and every secret in the host's secret store; plain HTTP redirects to HTTPS.
- Given a new image version, when it is deployed, then migrations run first (RF-1302) and the previous image tag stays available for rollback.
- Given the deployment, when it is live, then `docs/runbooks/deploy.md` describes how to deploy, roll back, back up and restore it.
- Given a production deploy, when its smoke test has passed, then the release workflow adds a comment to the open issue "Production deploys" (creating it when there is none) that mentions the owner and names the image tag, the commit, the public URL and the run, so GitHub emails the owner; a failure to comment never fails a deploy that is already live.
- Given a commit pushed to `main` (the release merge), when it lands, then a checks workflow builds, scans and pushes the image of that commit, the ZAP baseline and the staging check are confirmed, and the result is recorded on that commit; a check that fails comments on the open issue "Production deploys" so that the owner is told before the release hour, and nothing deploys.
- Given a commit pushed to `main` (the release merge), when the checks workflow `release-checks.yml` runs, then its job works out the tags with the script of Release (`sha-<short>` of that commit, never `latest`), builds the runtime image once, scans it with the Trivy gate of Release (a fixable High or Critical finding stops the job before anything is pushed) and pushes the tags to GHCR, with the same steps as the `image` job of `release.yml`; the job holds only `contents: read`, `checks: read`, `packages: write`, `statuses: write` and `issues: write` of the workflow token and reads no other token or secret.
- Given a commit pushed to `main` (the release merge), when `release-checks.yml` has pushed its image (GitHub starts it when the CI run of that commit completes, so no runner waits for the check `zap-baseline`, DEC-227), then its job confirms the ZAP baseline and the staging check with the steps "The ZAP baseline passed on this commit" and "This tree passed staging" of the `deploy` job of `release.yml`, word for word (the same rules, the repository variable `ROSETTA_STAGING_ENABLED` included; the workflow has no `skip-staging-check`); then, in every case, a failed, skipped or cancelled step included, it sets the commit status `release/ready` on that commit with the workflow token: `success` only when every step before it succeeded and both checks passed, otherwise `failure` with a description that names the check that failed or did not run (the image, the ZAP baseline or the staging check).
- Given a commit pushed to `main` (the release merge), when `release-checks.yml` has recorded `release/ready` as `failure` (or could not record it), whatever the cause, a failed, cancelled or timed-out step included, then its job comments on the open issue "Production deploys" (creating it when there is none) with the script and the issue lookup of the notification of Release, mentions the owner and names the commit, the cause from the description of `release/ready` (a `zap-baseline` that a later push to `main` cancelled, named as such; one that did not finish is named by the hourly sweep below, P8-143) and the run, so GitHub emails the owner before the release hour; it uses only the workflow token, and checks that pass comment nothing.
- Given a commit pushed to `main` (the release merge) that has no `release/ready`, when the hourly sweep of `release-checks.yml` finds that its `zap-baseline` had not finished 40 minutes after the merge, or had finished but nothing was recorded 2 hours after the merge, then the sweep records `release/ready` as `failure` with that cause named and comments on the open issue "Production deploys" as above, so a check that never completes still tells the owner (DEC-227). The sweep reads every page of a commit's statuses; a comment that fails does not stop it: it records and comments on every other merge it finds and then ends failed, so the run shows it (P8-143).
- Given 06:00 Europe/Madrid in summer or winter time, when the scheduler of ADR-028 starts Release on `main`, then Release takes the restore point, migrates, switches the image and runs the smoke test for the head commit only if all of its checks passed; otherwise it does nothing and names the check that is missing or failed.
- Given any other hour in Europe/Madrid, when the scheduler wakes at 04:00 or 05:00 UTC, then it starts nothing; the scheduler uses a token limited to this repository with Actions write, stored as a secret of the job, and reads no other secret.
  Verification: manual deployment; smoke test on the public URL (health, landing page, sign-in); `tests/integration/tooling/release-notify.test.ts` (the notification step); `tests/integration/docs/release-flow.test.ts` (the wording of the 06:00 start); `tests/integration/tooling/release-checks-workflow.test.ts` (the checks workflow); `tests/integration/tooling/release-checks-ready.test.ts` and `tests/integration/tooling/release-ready-status.test.ts` (the ZAP and staging confirmation and `release/ready`); `tests/integration/tooling/release-checks-notify.test.ts` (the failure comment of the checks); `tests/integration/tooling/release-checks-sweep.test.ts` (the sweep).

**RF-1305 Configuration from the environment** - R1 (M1) - Must - Source DEC-61 - ADR-014

- Given the server, when it starts, then it reads `DATABASE_URL`, `ROSETTA_SECRET_KEY`, `ROSETTA_DATA_DIR`, `ROSETTA_PUBLIC_URL`, the bootstrap administrator, optional server provider keys and log level from the environment (caps are not environment variables: they live in `server_settings` and are edited on the administration page, DEC-107), validates them with a schema, and stops with an error code naming any missing or invalid variable, never its value.
- Given `.env.example`, when it is read, then it lists every variable with a comment and a safe example value, and contains no real secret.
  Verification: unit tests on the configuration schema.

**RF-1306 Backups** - R1 - Should - Source DEC-65 - ADR-016

- Given the hosted deployment, when a day passes, then the database is backed up by the host (or a scheduled `pg_dump`) and kept 7 days; the data folder is backed up weekly; the runbook describes the restore and it has been tried once.
  Verification: one restore drill recorded in the runbook.

### 5.13 Prompt lab (RF-1400..RF-1499)

The prompt lab (DEC-231) tries one prompt at a time: `npm run lab` sends the real messages of one prompt, over the
inputs of a case set, to one model and measures the answers, with deterministic checks and, when asked, a judge (RF-1407). Like `npm run eval` it is opt-in and
costs money; no test and no CI job runs it against a real model (ADR-008, ADR-029).

**RF-1400 Run one prompt on its cases** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-003, ADR-007, ADR-008, ADR-029
As a developer I want to run one version of one prompt over a set of cases with one model so that I can measure it alone, not only inside a whole run.

- Given `npm run lab -- --prompt <id> --version <vN|file> --model <provider:model> --cases <set> [--repetitions n] --yes`, when it runs, then every case of the set is rendered into the prompt's real system prompt and messages by the same code the run uses, sent through the real adapter wrapped by the egress and budget guards, and one result per case and repetition is written to the git-ignored `lab-out/<id>/results.json`.
- Given `--version v3`, when the lab runs, then it uses the file `<id>.v3.md` of the prompts folder; given a path to a file, it uses that file instead (a draft not yet in `prompts/`), checked like any prompt file (no data block marker); the result records the file's name, version and SHA-256.
- Given a prompt id the lab has no target for, or a case set written for another prompt, when the command starts, then it stops with a message and sends nothing.
- Given the command without `--yes`, when it starts, then it only shows the prompt, the model, the number of calls and the caps, and sends nothing (RF-143).
  Verification: `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-run.test.ts`, `tests/unit/presentation/lab/lab-args.test.ts` (fake provider only).

**RF-1401 Case sets** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-029
As a developer I want a case to hold the inputs of a prompt, not its text, so that another version of the prompt can be rendered over the same inputs.

- Given a case set `lab/cases/<set>.json`, when it is read, then it is parsed strictly (unknown keys refused, every text limited): `schemaVersion` 1, a `version`, the `prompt` id it is written for, and cases with a unique `id`, an optional `expected` label, a `split` (`tune` or `held-back`, default `tune`) and the `input` the prompt's target defines; every problem is named with the case id and nothing is sent.
- Given a case of the verifier prompt, when it is rendered, then the claim and the lines it cites reach the model as labelled data blocks built by the verifier's own message builder (RF-145), over files the case holds, so no repository is read.
- Given a prompt id and its target, when a target is added to the registry, then the input schema of its cases, the call of the run's own message builder and the check of an answer are the only things it supplies.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-case-set.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets.test.ts`.

**RF-1402 Spend ledger and caps** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-007, ADR-029
As the product owner I want one ledger for the whole phase so that the lab can never spend past $50 and stops at $40 for me.

- Given `lab/config.json` (`totalCapMicros` 50 000 000, `stopMicros` 40 000 000, `invocationCapMicros`), when a call is about to be sent, then the lab appends a hold of the call's worst case to `lab-out/ledger.jsonl` and sends the call only when what the ledger holds up to that line, spent and held together, stays within the invocation cap, the total cap and the stop line, in that order; otherwise it refuses the call with RST-1600 and the reason, and the rest of the invocation is skipped.
- Given the environment variable `ROSETTA_LAB_LEDGER` set to an absolute path ending in `.jsonl`, when the lab runs, then it uses that file as the ledger instead of `lab-out/ledger.jsonl` and creates its folder if missing, so two builders in two checkouts share one phase total; a value that is not an absolute `.jsonl` path stops the command with RST-1000 and a plain message, before anything is sent.
- Given a model whose provider is not the fake provider of the tests, when `ROSETTA_LAB_LEDGER` is not set to a valid path, then the command refuses to start, with or without `--yes`, with RST-1000 and the plain message "set ROSETTA_LAB_LEDGER to the shared Phase 10 ledger": every real lab run of Phase 10 shares one ledger, so the $50 total and the $40 stop line cover all builders together (DEC-231). Tests with the fake provider keep the ledger of the checkout.
- Given provider settings whose reservation lifetime, as the budget guard counts it (every attempt's timeout, the longest wait between attempts and a margin), exceeds the 30 minutes a hold lasts, when the command starts, then it refuses with RST-1000 and a plain message naming the provider; a provider of kind `ollama` whose model is priced at zero is exempt, because its calls cost nothing and a hold that runs out loses no money (a priced Ollama model is checked like any other). A hold is not renewed while a call runs; the timeouts and waits bound a call that is answered in one piece, and for a streamed answer the timeout counts idle time, not the whole answer, so the check is a guard against a configuration that cannot fit and not a proof that no call lasts longer.
- Given a call that was answered, when its cost is known, then the hold is replaced by a line with the real cost, priced by the price table; a call that failed after it was sent is settled at the larger of the cost the budget guard knows and its held worst case, and only a call refused before sending releases its hold; when a hold, the settle line or the release line cannot be written the invocation stops (Refused) with a plain message, and nothing is sent without a hold.
- Given two lab commands that run at the same time, when both write to the ledger, then the file is append-only and each decides on the lines up to its own hold, so both together never pass a cap; a hold older than thirty minutes that was never settled stops counting.
- Given a phase total that has reached the stop line, when the lab runs again, then every call is refused naming the stop line, until the owner raises `stopMicros` in `lab/config.json`.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/spend-ledger.test.ts`, `tests/integration/infrastructure/lab/file-spend-ledger.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-caps.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-shared-ledger.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-hold-lifetime.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-write-failure.test.ts`, `tests/integration/infrastructure/lab/lab-call-gate.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/run-lab.test.ts`.

**RF-1403 Metrics and comparison** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - RNF-015
As a developer I want deterministic metrics per case and a side-by-side report so that a prompt version is kept only when it measurably beats the current one.

- Given a finished case, when its result is written, then it holds: valid on the first try, repaired (a later call was needed and gave a usable answer), refused (a guard or the ledger refused the call), the RST code of a failure, input and output tokens, latency, cost, the number of calls and, when the case has an `expected` label, whether the answer agreed.
- Given a target whose answer has no parser of the run (the first turn of a reader task: `reader/task`, `reader/area`, `reader/read-first`, `reader/answers`, `reader/reread`, `reader/parent-cards`, `reader/read-more`, whose answer may be tool calls that the answer text does not show), when a case is written, then its first-try validity is `null` (not applicable), never `true`; the summary's first-try rate counts only the cases it applies to and prints `n/a` when it applies to none, and a case of such a target is shown as `usable` or `not usable`; `detail` and the not-usable rate are the measure of a first turn (`results.json` keeps `schemaVersion` 1: the field only became nullable).
- Given a finished invocation, when its summary is printed, then it shows the rates of first-try validity, repairs, refusals and errors, the mean tokens and latency per call and the cost, and the date, model, model version and prompt SHA-256 are saved with it (RNF-015).
- Given `npm run lab -- --compare <runA> <runB>`, when both folders hold results of the same case set, then the report gives the two versions side by side on the same cases (totals and each case); two runs of different sets are refused. The comparison makes no call.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-compare.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-compare.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/run-lab.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-reader.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-reader-parts.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-run-captured.test.ts`.

**RF-1404 Capture lab cases from a run** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231, DEC-57 - ADR-029
As a developer I want a run's saved call log turned into lab cases so that every prompt is tried over the inputs it really got, not over inputs I invent.

- Given `npm run lab -- --capture --prompt <id> --run <source>=<folder> [--run ...] --sources <file> --name <set>`, when it runs, then it reads the `manifest.json` and `transcripts/` of each run folder (the run folder itself, or a folder that holds exactly one) and writes `lab/cases/<set>.json`; it makes no model call, reads no key and no `.env`, and changes no file of the run folder.
- Given the transcript of an agent task that belongs to the prompt id (the planner's gap call, a summariser merge call, the reader's tasks), when it is captured, then the case holds only the inputs the run sent, read back from the data blocks of the task's first messages: never the system prompt, never a model's answer, never the text of an assistant message.
- Given a task whose captured input holds an e-mail address, a drive path, a home folder or something that looks like a key, when it is captured, then that case is left out and the command says how many were left out; nothing personal reaches `lab/`.
- Given a captured input, when it is kept, then every text of it is masked again by the run's secret masker (RF-141, with the note of the masked-value prompt file), so a secret shape the masker learnt after the run was saved is masked in the case too.
- Given two tasks of the runs whose captured inputs are equal (the canonical JSON of the input: keys sorted, no case id), when the set is built, then only the first is kept, before the split, and the command names each one it dropped and the case it repeats.
- Given more candidate tasks than `--max-per-run`, when cases are chosen, then they are chosen by the order of a hash of the task name, never by what the input says, so the choice cannot favour an easy case.
- Given a prompt id the capture has no rule for, or a run folder that is not readable, when the command starts, then it stops with a message that names the ids it can capture, and writes nothing.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/data-blocks.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/capture-rules.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/build-case-set.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/held-back.test.ts`, `tests/integration/infrastructure/lab/run-folder.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-capture.test.ts` (synthetic run folders only).

**RF-1405 Committed case sets of published code, local sets for the rest** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231, DEC-57, DEC-03 - ADR-029
As a developer I want committed case sets of public code, with their source and licence, and a held-back part chosen before any tuning, so that a prompt version is judged on cases it was not tuned on.

- Given a case set, when it is read, then it may hold `sources` (a key, a name, the repository address, the source commit or the snapshot hash of the upload, the licence and the run ids the cases come from) and each case may name its source with `from`; a case that names a source the set does not list is refused.
- Given a set written by the capture, when it is written, then the side of a case is decided by its input alone: the SHA-256 of the canonical JSON of the input (keys sorted at every level, no case id and no prompt id), read as a number, is `held-back` when it is below a third of the hash space, and `tune` otherwise; no other input of the set is read, so one input has one side in every set that holds it, a set holds no input twice, and about a third of a set is held back. The rule reads no answer and no prompt version, so the split exists before any version is tried and the same input always gets the same side (ADR-029, amendments of the P10 lab fixes, which replaced the earlier rules on `<prompt id>:<case id>` and on a third of each set's own inputs).
- Given a set that the rule leaves with no `held-back` case or no `tune` case, when the capture writes it, then the set is written and the command prints a warning that names the missing side: no version can be kept on a set with no held-back case (DEC-231) and none tuned on a set with no tune case until more runs are captured.
- Given a set built by hand on a captured set (the repair sets of RF-1406), when it is written, then each case is on the side of the case it was built on, so the input of a held-back case is never tuned on through its repair.
- Given the committed sets of `lab/cases/`, built from runs of eShopModernizing (MIT) only, when the tests run, then every set has at least three cases, every set parses, every case is read by the target of its prompt, every set names a source with a licence for every case, every captured set follows the rule above, every repair case is on its base case's side, one input has one side in every committed set, only the sets named in the test (and in the runbook) lack a side, no set holds an input twice, no case holds a personal pattern or a text the run's secret masker would mask, and no case has an `expected` that a model wrote (an `expected` is written by hand from the code, DEC-57).
- Given inputs from code of unknown origin or licence (the Asset Manager and Invoice Generator workbooks), when the capture runs with `--local`, then the set is written to the git-ignored `lab/local-cases/`, which `--cases` reads when `lab/cases/` has no set of that name; no committed file names or quotes them (DEC-03).
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-case-set.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/held-back.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/committed-case-sets.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/repair-case-sets.test.ts`, `tests/integration/infrastructure/lab/lab-files.test.ts`.

**RF-1406 Lab targets for the planner, the summariser merge, the cards format and the reader** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-029
As a developer I want more of the prompts to be tried alone so that tuning cards can start with a target in place.

- Given the prompt `planner/gap`, when a case is run, then its items and plan are rendered by the run's own `gapMessage` and the answer is read by `parseGapAnswer`; `summariser/merge` is rendered by the run's `mergeMessage` and read by `parseMergeAnswer`.
- Given a reader prompt (`reader/task`, `reader/area`, `reader/read-first`) that drives a tool loop, when a case is run, then the lab measures the first turn only: the conversation up to that turn is built from the case with the run's own prompt builders (the area's file list as a data block, for `reader/read-first` also a fixed premature answer and the refusal), the file under test replaces its own part of the reader's prompts, one call is made with the reader's tool definitions, and the result is `ReadsFiles` (known tools, valid arguments), `BadToolCall`, `Answered` or `Empty`.
- Given the prompt `cards/card-format`, when a case is run, then the conversation is the task message, a replay of the reads the case holds (an assistant turn with the `read_file` calls and the data blocks the tool gave) and the turns-left message, one call is made, and the answer is read by the run's card parser: `Valid`, `Partial` (cards and problems) or `Unusable`.
- Given a reader prompt that is a part of a turn (`reader/answers`, `reader/reread`, `reader/parent-cards`, `reader/read-more`, `reader/turns-left`, `reader/workbook`), when a case is run, then the file under test replaces its own part of the reader's prompts, the run's own builders make the turn and the other parts are left out: the owner's answers, the rejected claims or the parent's cards are added to the first message as the data block the case holds (first-turn kinds); the read-more refusal follows the replay of the case's reads and a fixed answer (first-turn kinds); the turns-left message ends the replay with one turn left and the workbook guidance is added to the system prompt of a workbook case, both read by the run's card parser (`Valid`, `Partial`, `Unusable`, `ReadsMore`); a draft without the placeholder of its prompt is `BadPrompt` before any call.
- Given a plan document prompt (`planner/data-model`, `planner/prd`, `planner/api-contract`, `planner/ux`, `planner/flows`, `planner/coverage`, `planner/package`, `planner/workbook`, `planner/adr-model`), when a case is run, then its cards and owner answers are rendered by the run's own message builder (`dataModelMessageFor`, the plan message functions, `apiContractMessage`, `uxMessage`, `flowsMessage`, `coverageMessage`, `adrModelMessage`) and the one answer is read by the run's own parser, with no domain check, repair or continuation; `planner/prd` is captured from the task `planner-prd`, `planner/package` and `planner/workbook` share the task `planner-package`, and `planner/workbook` is the package message sent under the run's package prompt followed by the file under test (`withWorkbookGuidance`), so its set is local only; `planner/adr-model` has a target and a capture rule but no set yet, as no saved run holds its call.
- Given a card prompt that is not the card format (`cards/flows`, `cards/screens`, `cards/continue`), when a case is run, then the case is a reader case that holds reads, the conversation is the task message and the replay of the reads, and one call is made with the reader's tool definitions; for `cards/flows` and `cards/screens` the file under test is added after the card format of the system prompt as the run adds it for a marked area, with the other fragment left out, and the answer is read by the run's card parser (`Valid`, `Partial`, `Unusable`, `ReadsMore`); for `cards/continue` the conversation goes on with a fixed cut answer (`{"cards": [{"type": "BR", "title": "`, never a model's) and the file under test as the next user message, and the fragment the model sends is joined to the cut answer by the run's `joinFragment` before the same parser reads it; the capture takes `cards/continue` from any reader task that read a file and `cards/flows` or `cards/screens` only from a task whose system prompt held that fragment (read from its heading, nothing else of the prompt is kept).
- Given a planner repair prompt (`planner/gap-repair`, `planner/plan-repair`), when a case is run, then the case is the input of the call it repairs with the problem lines the run writes (for the gap: position, field and fixed reason; for the plan: list, position when there is one, field and the reason of an item the check left out), the run's own builder renders the repair message (`gapRepairMessage`, `planRepairMessage` after the plan message: the data blocks, then the file under test, then one line per problem), the call is sent under the run's own system prompt of the call it repairs (the gap prompt, the package prompt), and the one answer is read by the run's parser of that call (`Cards`, `NoCards`, `Unusable`; `Tasks`, `NoTasks`, `Unusable`); a case with no problem is refused.
- Given the prompt `cards/repair`, when a case is run, then the case is a card-format case with an earlier answer written for the case (never a model's), the conversation is the task message, the replay of the reads, that answer as the assistant's turn and the repair request the run builds from the problems the run's card parser finds in it (`renderRepairRequest`, with the file under test in place of the reader's repair prompt), one call is made with the reader's tool definitions and the answer is read by the card parser (`Valid`, `Partial`, `Unusable`, `ReadsMore`); a case without reads or with an answer the parser finds no problem in is refused, and a draft without `{{problems}}` is `BadPrompt` before any call.
- Given a repair prompt, when a case set is captured, then the capture has no rule for it: a planner task's transcript does not hold the repair message and its problem lines come from the refused answer, and a card repair follows a model's answer; the committed sets `planner-gap-repair`, `planner-plan-repair` and `cards-repair` are built by hand on the committed eShopModernizing sets of the call they repair, and each case is on the side of the case it was built on (RF-1405).
- Given the prompt `shared/masked-value`, when a case is run, then the case is a reader case with reads whose lines name an invented secret as the token `{secret}`, the value being in the case field `secret` (so no value stands in the committed set), the lab puts the value in, masks the reads with the run's own masker using the file under test as its replacement (every line stays where it was), sends the task and the replay of the reads in one call that must be the answer (P10-10: the replay ends with the turns-left message of the last turn, one turn left, and the call carries no tools (a real run keeps its tools on the last turn, `agent-loop.ts`; the lab drops them on purpose), because a model that asks to read more leaves no text for the check, which measures what the prompt is for and not the model's next move), and reads the answer with the run's card parser (`Valid`, `Partial`, `Unusable`; a tool call written as text is `Unusable`); a value still in the text sent or repeated in the answer (its text or the arguments of a tool call) is `Leaked` (before any call in the first case), and a draft without `{{kind}}` or whose note does not start with `[MASKED:` is `BadPrompt` before any call. A version with a `Leaked` case is never kept: a missed secret is a regression no score can buy back.
- Given the prompt `shared/text-tool-protocol`, when a case is run, then the file under test is added after the reader's system prompt with the tool definitions in it, as the Ollama adapter adds it for a model without native tool calling, the request carries no native tools, the replay of the case's reads travels as `tool_call` and `tool_result` blocks in user and assistant messages, one call is made and the answer is read by the run's text parser (`parseTextAnswer`): as a first turn (`ReadsFiles`, `BadToolCall`, `Answered`, `Empty`) for a case without reads, as cards (`Valid`, `Partial`, `Unusable`, `ReadsMore`) for a case with reads; first-try validity is not applicable; a draft without the run's tools placeholder (one or more are accepted, as the run does) is `BadPrompt` before any call.
- Given the prompt `shared/secret-risk`, when the lab is asked for it, then it has no target: the domain fills its text with the path and the lines and no model is sent it, so there is no call to run and no answer to read; the runbook says `no call` for it.
- Given the committed sets `shared-masked-value` and `shared-text-tool-protocol`, when the tests run, then each is built by hand from invented code (no source, no licence), has at least eight cases with a label each, is split by the fixed rule of RF-1405 on the input, holds no input twice and is read case by case by its target; the masked-value set holds only values of the form `FakeSecret<n>` in the case field `secret` (never a real key shape), names them in the lines only as `{secret}`, and the run's masker hides each value, in at least four kinds, so the masker's own test is run on the set apart from the general check of RF-1405 that no committed text is masked.
- Given the runbook, when the tests run, then it lists every live prompt family of `prompts/` with its state, `wired`, `not yet` or `no call` (a prompt no model is sent), the list agrees with the targets the lab has, and the count of wired prompt ids in its first section equals the rows.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-planner.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-reader.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-reader-parts.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/capture-reader-parts.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/runbook-prompt-list.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-run-captured.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/plan-docs/plan-doc-targets.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/plan-docs/plan-doc-round-trip.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/plan-doc-case-sets.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/repairs/repair-targets.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/repairs/repair-round-trip.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/repairs/cards-repair-target.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/repair-case-sets.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-card-surface.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/capture/capture-card-surface.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-targets-shared.test.ts`, `tests/integration/lab/shared-case-sets.test.ts`.

**RF-1407 Judge a lab answer against a rubric** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-007, ADR-029
As a developer I want a judge to score an answer against a written rubric of its prompt family so that quality the deterministic metrics cannot see is measured, and a prompt version is kept on evidence.

- Given `lab/rubrics/<family>.md` for each family (reader, verifier, summariser, planner, cards, shared), when it is read, then it holds criteria as `## <id> - <title>` headings with the guidance for scores 1 to 5, a criterion `grounding` (the answer says only what the given source shows) in every family, a `[veto]` mark on a criterion whose score 1 makes the overall score 1 (the missed secret of the `shared` rubric), and the criterion `verdict-correct` in the verifier's (agreement with the hand label), also a `[veto]`: a wrong verdict makes the overall score 1; a rubric without `grounding`, with a repeated id or with fewer than three criteria is refused before any call.
- Given `npm run lab -- ... --judge <provider:model> --yes`, when a case has been answered, then one judge call scores the last answer against the rubric of the prompt's family (the folder of the prompt id), with the judge prompt `prompts/lab/judge.v1.md` as the system prompt followed by the rubric; the case's inputs, the answer and the case's `expected` label reach the judge only as three labelled data blocks (`lab_source`, `lab_answer`, `lab_label`), each with its own random tag, because they are untrusted text (RF-145).
- Given a judge reply, when it is read, then it must be one JSON object with a score from 1 to 5 and a short reason for every criterion of the rubric and for no other; a reply that is not is recorded as `unreadable` and the case has no judge score. The overall score is computed by the lab, not the judge: the mean of the criteria to one decimal, or 1 when a `[veto]` criterion scored 1.
- Given a judge call, when it is sent, then it goes through the same guarded provider, ledger gate and shared ledger as every lab call (RF-1402): it counts in the invocation cap, the stop line and the $50, and a refused judge call stops the invocation (Refused) like any other. A judge call that fails after it was sent (for example a rejected key or an unknown model) also stops it, with its RST code in the case's `judge.rstCode`, because a call that failed after sending is booked at its worst case and a broken judge would otherwise be booked on every case. Every judge call that was sent counts in `judge.calls`, answered or not. Its tokens and cost are recorded apart from the target's (`judge.costMicros`).
- Given a judged run, when `results.json` is written, then each case holds its criterion scores with reasons, its overall score and the judge's cost, and the run records the judge model, the judge prompt and the rubric, each with its SHA-256; the summary prints the mean overall and per criterion and the judge cost, and `--compare` shows them side by side per case and in the totals. Without `--judge` nothing changes and no call is added.
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/judge/`, `tests/unit/presentation/lab/lab-args.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-judge.test.ts`, `tests/unit/domain/egress/data-block-lab-sources.test.ts`.

**RF-1408 Check the judge and sample it for the owner** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-029
As the product owner I want to know how far the judge can be trusted, and to see its scores, so that I decide whether a judge score may rule a prompt version in or out.

- Given `npm run lab -- --judge-check <provider:model> [--limit n] [--yes]` (`--limit n` scores only the first n items of each kind and family, for a smoke run), when it runs, then without `--yes` it shows the number of judge calls and the caps and sends nothing; with `--yes` the judge scores (a) a fixed sample of the hand-labelled calibration claims of `eval/calibration/` (RF-305), each shown with the verdict the hand label gives or its opposite, alternately, without telling the judge the label and with one of two fixed reasons (so a flipped verdict cannot be told by its reason), and (b) the hand-scored samples of `eval/judge-check/hand-scored.json`, at least three per family, written from public eShopModernizing code (MIT licence) or invented material, each with its source and licence named.
- Given the calls are done, when the report is written (`lab-out/<id>/judge-check.json`, git-ignored) and printed, then it gives the agreement of the judge with the hand labels on the calibration sample (the judge's `verdict-correct` score of 4 or 5 exactly when the shown verdict follows the label) and with the hand scores on each family and criterion (exact, within one point, mean absolute difference) and the agreement of the overall score by the same rule; every call goes through the ledger gate (RF-1402).
- Given the judge check and the judged runs of the card, when the owner reviews them, then `docs/evaluation/prompt-lab/judge-sample.md` holds a sample of the judge's scores per family with short quotes and the figures, never long model answers.
  Verification: `tests/integration/presentation/lab/judge-check.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/judge/judge-check-set.test.ts`.

**RF-1409 Run one side or a sample of a case set** - R2 (Phase 10) - Must - Source DEC-231 - ADR-029
As a developer I want to send only the tune cases, only the held-back cases or a small fixed sample of a set so that a baseline on several models costs what its budget allows and two runs stay comparable.

- Given `npm run lab -- ... --split tune` (or `--split held-back`), when the run is planned, then only the cases of that side are sent (the plan counts them), and a value other than `tune` or `held-back` is refused before anything is sent.
- Given `--sample <n>` (a whole number from 1 to 500), when the run is planned, then the cases of the side asked (all of them without `--split`) are sorted by case id in plain code-unit order, whatever the order of the set, and the first n are sent, so a sample is the same on every run, a smaller sample is the start of a larger one (a quarter of the cases lies inside a half), and an n above the cases left sends them all. Without `--sample` the cases keep the order of the set.
- Given a selection that holds no case (for example `--split held-back` on a set with none), when the command starts, then it prints that there is nothing to send and sends nothing, with or without `--yes`.
- Given a run made with these options, when `results.json` is written, then it records `selection { split: tune | held-back | all, sample: n | null }` (`all` and `null` when the option is absent), `planned` counts the selected cases, and `caseSet.sha256` is still the hash of the whole file.
- Given `--compare a b` of two runs whose `selection` differs (a results file with no `selection` counts as `all` and no sample), when it starts, then it refuses, naming both selections, and sends nothing, as it refuses two case sets (RF-1403).
  Verification: `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-selection.test.ts`, `tests/unit/presentation/lab/lab-args.test.ts`, `tests/unit/application/lab/lab-compare.test.ts`, `tests/integration/presentation/lab/lab-selection.test.ts`.

## 6. Non-functional requirements

| Id      | Requirement                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Verification                                                                                        |
| ------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| RNF-001 | **Portability**: the server runs as a Linux container image (amd64) locally and hosted; development from source works on Windows, macOS and Linux with the baseline Node.js LTS; citations always use forward slashes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | CI on Windows and Linux; image built in CI; manual check on the owner's Windows machine with Docker |
| RNF-002 | **Robustness**: an interrupted run (Stop, crash, container restart, cap) leaves its run folder consistent: finished cards, the manifest end state and the cost so far are on disk.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | integration test that interrupts a run                                                              |
| RNF-003 | **Secrets**: no API key, token or masked secret is ever written to the repository, the output folder or a log; server keys come only from environment variables or a git-ignored `.env`; user keys are stored only encrypted in the database (RF-1104).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | test that scans outputs for key patterns; secret scanning on the public repository                  |
| RNF-004 | **Reproducibility**: every run records the Rosetta version, the code map hash, prompt versions with a SHA-256 of each prompt file, provider, the exact model version the provider reports (dated id; for Ollama the model digest) and parameters per role, so it can be explained and repeated (DEC-57).                                                                                                                                                                                    | integration test on the manifest                                                                    |
| RNF-005 | **Offline tests**: the full automated suite passes with no network, using the fake provider and recorded responses; core logic coverage is at least 80%.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | CI with network disabled; coverage report                                                           |
| RNF-006 | **Measured quality**: a golden set of hand-checked findings for the demo app scores each evaluated run; R1 targets at least 90% precision of `Supported` claims and at least 70% recall of golden business rules with a cloud verifier; local-model results are measured and reported, not targeted.                                                                                                                                                                                        | `npm run eval` against the golden set                                                               |
| RNF-007 | **Performance**: `scan` of the demo app takes under 10 seconds and of a 100,000-line repository under 2 minutes on the owner's machine.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | timing in the scan summary                                                                          |
| RNF-008 | **Usability**: every page explains its main action in one sentence; every error shows a code, a plain message and the next step.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | review of the pages; error-mapper tests                                                             |
| RNF-009 | **Licensing**: Rosetta is MIT-licensed; any third-party code used as a fixture is a small excerpt with its source and licence cited.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | review at each phase exit                                                                           |
| RNF-010 | **Accessibility**: the web UI, the landing page and the HTML report meet WCAG 2.1 AA (from P1 for the pages that exist then, DEC-112) for contrast, keyboard navigation and headings, in light and dark themes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | automated accessibility check plus manual keyboard test                                             |
| RNF-011 | **Local-first**: in the local deployment, once a snapshot is cached, the whole pipeline works with Ollama only and no internet connection.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | end-to-end run with the network disabled, a cached snapshot and Ollama running                      |
| RNF-012 | **Estimate accuracy**: the actual cost of an `understand` run falls within 30% of the likely estimate on the demo app.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | comparison of `estimate` and `cost.json` over three runs                                            |
| RNF-013 | **Clean Architecture**: the domain and application layers import nothing from infrastructure or presentation; dependencies point inward only; checked automatically on every pull request.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | dependency rule check in `npm run verify` (ADR-009)                                                 |
| RNF-014 | **Secure by default**: with only the required environment variables set, Rosetta requires sign-in for everything except the landing page, sign-in and health check, publishes its local port on `127.0.0.1` only, uses secure cookies, asks before any cloud egress, applies the server caps, masks secrets, applies the built-in deny list, masks transcripts and sends no telemetry; weakening any of these needs an explicit configuration entry that the run records (DEC-57, ADR-013). | integration test with `.env.example`; review of the defaults table in architecture section 10       |
| RNF-015 | **Evaluations are experiments**: every `npm run eval` result is saved with its date, models and model versions, prompt hashes and settings; each golden case runs at least 3 times and the report shows the spread; the golden set includes cases whose correct answer is "not in the code" (DEC-57).                                                                                                                                                                                       | evaluation output schema test                                                                       |
| RNF-016 | **Availability for evaluation**: the hosted deployment is reachable from 2026-10-26 until the master's evaluation ends (Q-01), with a monthly server cap that cannot be exceeded and a health check the host restarts on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | host health checks; budget banner (RF-1106)                                                         |
| RNF-017 | **Hosted performance**: with two runs in progress, pages answer in under 1 second and the live view receives events within 2 seconds of their creation.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | measured once on the hosted deployment before the hand-in                                           |

| -------------- | ----------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| RF-700..RF-799 | out of scope (DEC-60; revisit after 2026-10-26) | Claude Code plugin run mode: the same prompts and card formats as skills and subagents, writing the same output folder, so a final run can use a Claude subscription |
| RF-900..RF-999 | R2                                              | Multi-target evaluation and provider comparison: a second demo target in another stack (Q-05) and a report comparing quality and cost per provider and model         |
